# HISTORY — Die V1

This file holds the full CONFIRMED WORKING record, verbatim, one paragraph per build, in build order. It is not read at session start. CLAUDE.md's CONFIRMED WORKING section carries only a one-line summary per build; come here when a specific build's full detail — exact numbers, exact code paths, exact Playwright verification steps — is needed.

Moved out of CLAUDE.md and into this file on BUILD 099 (Stage 2.30, the documentation rewrite), verbatim and in the original order, as CLAUDE.md's standing spec sections were rewritten to state current truth and this log was compacted to one line per build. Nothing below this point was reworded.

---

Stage 0.1 — HTML shell and gameState object. Schema correct, five helpers working, getCard() working, init runs on load, all 40 die faces initialised, deck shuffled.
Stage 0.2 — Phase machine skeleton. All seven phases fire in order and loop correctly.
Stage 0.3 — Listener registration system. registerListener, callListeners, clearListeners working.
Stage 0.4 — State mutation helpers and state inspector.
Stage 0.5 — Init divider log line.
UI — Two-column layout. Enemy panel top, player panel bottom, middle strip, log right column colour-coded by prefix.
Stage 1.2 — Config sync. gameState player hp/maxHp 70, enemy hp/maxHp 45 intent 10. config.cards holds exactly strike, ward, rite (draw and discard_draw removed). config.classes holds only ordained. Ordained startingDeck (5 strike, 4 ward, 1 rite) drives gameState.player.deck, shuffled. init() logs summary lines instead of full array dumps.
Stage 1.3 — Config sync repair and build stamp. Player/enemy stat panels and hand-row card buttons now render live from gameState/config.cards instead of hardcoded HTML (renderStats, renderCardButtons, wired into refreshInspector). Confirmed 70/70 player, 45/45 + intent 10 enemy, exactly 3 card buttons (Strike, Ward, Rite). updatePlayer/updateEnemy/updateTurn/updateDie/updateRun now accept an optional silent flag so init()'s full-array die/deck writes no longer duplicate-log the raw arrays; only the [INIT] summary lines remain. Build stamp added top-left (fixed position, #4a3f2a, 10px) — update stage number and build time on every future substage.
Stage 1.4 — Enemy roll, blank face passive, and MOD_TRIGGER verification. Build stamp moved into normal document flow (no more overlap) and switched to a build counter (BUILD 004, increment on every future edit, no dates/times). ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE now rolls gameState.enemy.die.faces and routes to ENEMY_NAT_ONE / ENEMY_BUFF_TRIGGER / blank-log / Nat-20-stub. Ordained's onBlankRoll (generateBlock(3), updatePlayer, ON_BLOCK_GENERATED source 'blank_face', [BLANK] log) is registered as a permanent BLANK_ROLL listener during class setup in init(), not inline in the phase machine. Added a generic permanent MOD_TRIGGER dispatcher that resolves config.mods[modId] and calls its effect. Added config.mods['dev_test'] (DEV ONLY — REMOVE WHEN REAL MODS EXIST) on player die face 10. Also added renderDieList()/modDisplayName() so the die-face panels render live from gameState instead of static HTML, since face 10 needed to visibly show "Dev Test".
Stage 1.5 — Blank roll passive bug fix. Root cause: calculateDamage() and generateBlock() were referenced by card effects and onBlankRoll since Stage 1.2 but never actually defined anywhere in index.html — calling onBlankRoll() threw an uncaught ReferenceError before it reached the [BLANK] log or the updatePlayer call, silently killing the block increase. Registration, callListeners('BLANK_ROLL', {}) in ROLL_PHASE, and clearListeners (never called at all, so not the cause) were all already correct. Added calculateDamage()/generateBlock() exactly per the DAMAGE PIPELINE spec (flat additions then multipliers, Math.ceil throughout, reading DAMAGE_FLAT_ADDITION/DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER/BLOCK_FLAT_ADDITION/BLOCK_MULTIPLIER off the registry). Added a permanent [LISTENER] registered: <hook> <id> log inside registerListener() for all future registrations, and a temporary [ROLL] pool size: N log inside rollDie() (no change to the selection algorithm itself). Build stamp now STAGE 1.5 / BUILD 005.
Stage 1.6 — START_OF_TURN behaviour. Implemented in runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch in exact order: poison tick (only logs/damages if poisonStacks > 0, stacks unchanged), block clears to 0 via updatePlayer (always logs even from 0), soul resets to maxSoul via updatePlayer, clearListeners('turn'). Card drawing deliberately not implemented. Added gameState.turn.round (starts at 0, incremented at the top of every START_OF_TURN so the first turn shows Round 1) and wired it into the mid-strip via a live #roundValue span. Added a live Soul row to the player stat panel (id playerSoulValue, X / maxSoul format matching HP). Build stamp now STAGE 1.6 / BUILD 006.
Stage 1.7 — Card drawing and deck cycling. Added drawCards(n): draws from the top of deck into hand one at a time via updatePlayer, logging [DRAW] drew CardName; on an empty deck mid-draw it reshuffles discard into deck (logs [DRAW] discard reshuffled into deck: N cards) and continues; if both are empty it logs [DRAW] deck and discard both empty, cannot draw and stops entirely. START_OF_TURN now calls drawCards(5) as its final step, after clearListeners('turn'). END_PLAYER_TURN moves all of hand into discard, empties hand, and logs [END] discarded N cards to discard pile. renderCardButtons() now renders one button per card in gameState.player.hand (not per config.cards entry), labelled "Name (soulCost)"; duplicates render as separate buttons; buttons remain disabled — no play/soul-spend logic yet. Deck/Discard counts in the player panel were already live from Stage 1.3's renderStats(). Build stamp now STAGE 1.7 / BUILD 007. Phase-badge bug fix within this substage (BUILD 008): the phase machine (PHASE_ORDER/nextPhase/runPhase) was always correct — added renderPhaseBadge() since the mid-strip badge was static HTML never wired to gameState.turn.phase. Re-verified with no further code change in BUILD 009 (one confirmed code path, no duplicates).
Stage 1.8 — Card play. Added playCard(handIndex): checks gameState.player.soul against the card's soulCost; if insufficient, logs [CARD] not enough soul for CardName (cost N, have M) and returns with no state change; if sufficient, subtracts cost via updatePlayer, runs card.effect(gameState) (unchanged strike/ward/rite effects — damage through calculateDamage(), block through generateBlock(), matching the pipeline the blank-roll passive already uses), splices that exact hand index out of hand and appends the card id to discard via updatePlayer, then logs [CARD] played CardName (cost N). renderCardButtons() now builds each button with a click handler bound to its current index (rebuilt fresh every render, so indices never go stale) instead of being permanently disabled; also logs [CLICK] CardName per the existing all-buttons-log-clicks convention. Playing a card only touches player/enemy state via the existing helpers — it never calls runPhase/nextPhase, so it does not advance the turn; multiple plays per CARD_PHASE work as long as soul allows. Build stamp now STAGE 1.8 / BUILD 010.
Stage 1.9 — Enemy action. ENEMY_ACT_PHASE now computes rawDamage = max(0, intent - block), passes it through calculateDamage() per Law 1, and either logs [ENEMY] attack fully blocked (intent M, block B) with no state change, or applies the damage via updatePlayer({hp: ...}) and logs [ENEMY] dealt N damage (intent M, block B). Single hit only — no intent randomisation or multi-attack, out of scope. No new block-clear was added; the only clear remains START_OF_TURN's existing updatePlayer({block: 0}). HP/block re-render is automatic since updatePlayer already calls refreshInspector() internally. CHECK_WIN_LOSS win/loss handling still not implemented — deliberately deferred. Build stamp now STAGE 1.9 / BUILD 011.
Stage 1.10 — Win and loss. CONFIRMED. Manual browser pass verified: enemy HP crossed 0 via repeated Strike plays, VICTORY banner rendered correctly, Next Phase and hand buttons went inert on halt, clicking Next Phase after the halt does nothing, zero console errors.
Stage 1.10 playtest fix (BUILD 013, same substage, not a new stage). Bug one: CHECK_WIN_LOSS was only ever evaluated when that specific named phase was reached, which per the fixed PHASE_ORDER is last in the cycle — so HP crossing zero during CARD_PHASE let END_PLAYER_TURN/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ACT_PHASE all still run before the check caught it. PHASE_ORDER itself was not reordered (it's NEVER CHANGE). Instead, added a win/loss guard at the very top of runPhase(), evaluated on every single phase-transition attempt regardless of which phase is being entered: if run.status is still 'active' and enemy.hp <= 0 or player.hp <= 0, it sets status via updateRun, logs [WIN]/[LOSS], and returns immediately before the requested phase's own logic (or even its [PHASE] log line) executes. This means the instant a card kills the enemy in CARD_PHASE, the next Next-Phase click (attempting to enter END_PLAYER_TURN) halts right there — no further enemy phases run. The old duplicate win/loss check inside the phase === 'CHECK_WIN_LOSS' block was now dead code (the top guard always catches it first) and was removed, leaving only the awaiting-result log for the case both sides are still alive. Bug two: playCard() now checks gameState.turn.phase !== 'CARD_PHASE' first and, if true, logs [CARD] cannot play CardName outside CARD_PHASE and returns with no state change; hand buttons remain visually enabled outside CARD_PHASE (per spec) but are inert. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 013.
Stage 1.10 UI pass (BUILD 014, CSS/markup only, no game logic touched — verified all edits landed above the <script> tag). Added a .hero-stats block inside each stats-col: HP (♥) and Intent (⚔) for enemy; HP (♥), Block (🛡), and Soul (💧) for player — each at 32px bold with a 22px icon, separated from the secondary stat-lines (Poison Stacks, Loaded buffs, Active this turn/debuffs, Deck, Discard, which stay at the original 11px density) by a hairline border. Same element ids (enemyHpValue, enemyIntentValue, playerHpValue, playerBlockValue, playerSoulValue, etc.) were reused inside the new markup, so renderStats() needed no changes. .combat-panel padding bumped to 16px and die-col/stats-col gap to 24px; .stats-col widened 150px→200px to fit the larger HP text. .hand-row given 12px gap/padding and .hand-card given larger padding (14px/20px), 15px font, 44px min-height for comfortable click targets. Die face lists, log panel, and phase badge were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 014.
Stage 1.10 UI pass two (BUILD 015). Enemy/player panels now flex:1 inside .left-col (which is height:100%) so they fill the viewport instead of stopping partway down; die-row and face-btn/die-mod sizes roughly doubled (face-btn 20px→38px, die-mod 11px→19px), with .die-row given flex:1 so all 20 rows always fill whatever vertical room the panel has — no per-list scrolling, no faces hidden. Stat icon and value font-size unified at 38px so they read as one unit; Intent got a new .stat-hero-main wrapper with a live sublabel (id enemyIntentLabel, "Attacks for N" read from gameState.enemy.intent in renderStats() — no hardcoding). #enemyPanel/#playerPanel given --enemy-panel-bg (#120a0a) / --player-panel-bg (#0a0c12) for a faint red/blue cast, existing border colours untouched. Added --game-font (Bahnschrift → Arial Narrow → Segoe UI Semibold → Franklin Gothic Medium → Arial → sans-serif, all web-safe/no external load) applied only to panel headings, stat numbers, the round chip, phase badge, result banner, roll-hero text, and hand-card text — die face lists, #log, and #inspectorContent were left on the existing monospace stack (body's font-family was never changed, so log/inspector needed no override). Mid-strip rebuilt as the centrepiece: a new #rollHero shows the rolled face number large and central (72px) with the mod name / BLANK / NAT 1 / NAT 20 (gold, existing --nat colour) beneath it, Round chip and phase badge moved to a smaller supporting row above; a neutral "AWAITING ROLL" empty state shows before the first roll of a turn. This required two small read-only additions, not new game logic: log() now pattern-matches its own already-emitted "[PHASE] START_OF_TURN" (→ resetRollHero()) and "[ROLL] face: N modId: X" (→ renderRollResult(), parsed via regex) messages purely as a rendering hook — runPhase()/the phase machine/rollDie were not touched, this only observes text log() was already producing. A ~180ms scale/opacity pulse plays on the hero number each new roll. Hand cards rebuilt from plain buttons into real card elements (cost badge corner, name, short effect text via a display-only CARD_EFFECT_TEXT lookup — strike "6 damage", ward "5 block", rite "6 damage 6 block") in a non-wrapping row sized to fit a hand of five; cards the player can't currently afford (soul check against card.soulCost, display-only, does not alter playCard()'s own gate) get a dimmed .unaffordable class but stay clickable so the existing "not enough soul" log line still fires. Log panel CSS block (#log, #inspectorContent, .log-* colour classes) is byte-for-byte unchanged — confirmed by re-reading that section after all edits. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 015.
Stage 1.10 UI pass three (BUILD 016). Stat columns: .stats-col changed from a fixed 220px to flex:1 (die-col flex:1.1) so stats now use the panel's full width instead of clustering top-left; hero-stat icon/value bumped 38px→50px, and both .stat-hero rows and .stat-line rows now get a border-bottom divider each (last-child's divider removed) so every stat reads as its own row. --enemy-panel-bg/--player-panel-bg strengthened to #241111/#11152b (clearly perceptible red/blue while staying dark) and new --enemy-border (#5a2424) / --player-border (#26315a) applied via border-color on #enemyPanel/#playerPanel so the border tints match. Secondary-text contrast raised by repurposing --blank from #6b5a3a to #9c8a63 and applying it to: #buildStamp text colour (was a near-invisible #4a3f2a, now #9c8a63 to match), .stat-label, .round-chip's base colour, and .face-btn's default (blank-face) colour — die-mod already read --blank so it brightened automatically. The log panel's own dim-prefix hierarchy ([LISTENER]/[STATE]/[CLICK] etc.) was left untouched, confirmed unchanged in CONFIRMED WORKING BUILD 015 log block, re-verified byte-identical here too. Start Game/Next Phase centred via .controls-buttons {justify-content:center} (it stretches full-width as a flex column child by default) and enlarged via a new #startGameBtn, #nextPhaseBtn rule (17px game-font, 16px/40px padding) that doesn't touch the generic `button` rule, so #inspectorToggleBtn stays exactly as small as before. Card-play feedback: log() gained a third read-only pattern-match, on its own already-emitted "[CARD] played CardName (cost N)" lines, calling triggerCardFx(cardName) — this looks the card up in config.cards by name (display-only lookup) and, via a CARD_FX_TYPE map (strike=attack, ward=block, rite=both), toggles a 300ms #enemyPanel shake + red HP-number pop for attacks and a 350ms #playerPanel blue box-shadow pulse + blue Block-number pop for blocks — none of this touches playCard()/card.effect()/calculateDamage()/generateBlock(), it only reacts to text log() already produces. renderStats() also independently diffs gameState.enemy.hp/gameState.player.hp against tracked lastEnemyHp/lastPlayerHp module vars and pops the changed HP number red on any change (poison, enemy attack, card damage, etc.), not just card plays — all animations are pure CSS (@keyframes fxShake/fxPulseBlue/fxPopRed/fxPopBlue), synchronous classList toggles via a shared flashElement() helper, all ≤350ms, none block input. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 016, colour lifted to match the rest of the contrast pass.
Stage 1.10 mechanics update (BUILD 017): Consecrate rewritten. config.mods['consecrate'].effect now grants +2 soul immediately via updatePlayer (MOD_TRIGGER fires in ROLL_PHASE, always before CARD_PHASE, so the soul is spendable that turn) and registers a clearOn:'turn' listener on ON_CARD_PLAY that adds generateBlock(3) block per card played, firing ON_BLOCK_GENERATED with source 'consecrate' and logging [MOD] consecrate: 3 block for CardName each time — this covers cards played with the granted soul too, since it's a live listener, not a one-shot count. Turn-scoping reuses the existing clearListeners('turn') call already in START_OF_TURN, no new expiry logic needed; the soul itself never persists either, since START_OF_TURN unconditionally resets soul to maxSoul regardless of what it was. One necessary addition beyond the mod definition itself: ON_CARD_PLAY was a documented EVENT HOOK that nothing had ever actually fired — playCard() now calls callListeners('ON_CARD_PLAY', { card: card }) right after card.effect(gameState) runs. Without this the hook (and therefore Consecrate, and any future card-reactive mod) could not function at all; this is completing an already-specified wire, not new game rules. dev_test is untouched, still on player die face 10. config.mods now holds dev_test and consecrate; consecrate is not auto-placed on any die face — manual placement for testing is on the user. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 017.
Stage 1.10 fixes (BUILD 018): (1) config.mods['dev_test'] deleted entirely (grep-confirmed zero remaining references) now that a real mod exists to test with; player die face 10 now carries 'consecrate' instead, making it directly rollable. (2) Die face clipping fixed on both dice: root cause was .face-btn having a fixed height:38px while its row (.die-row, flex:1 with min-height:0 so 20 rows can share whatever vertical room the panel has) resolves to a computed height that can be less than 38px depending on viewport — with the row's align-items:center, the fixed-height button then overflowed above/below its row's box and visually overlapped neighbouring rows, which is what read as clipped/truncated digits. Fix: .face-btn now uses align-self:stretch with no explicit height, so it always exactly matches its row's actual computed height regardless of viewport — die-mod (the label span) was untouched and still centres via the row's own align-items:center. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 018.
Stage 1.10 dev tool — force roll (BUILD 019). ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE's inline roll-resolution logic was extracted unchanged into resolvePlayerRoll(face)/resolveEnemyRoll(face) (same log lines, same callListeners calls, same branching, nothing reordered or shortcut); runPhase()'s natural-roll blocks now just call rollDie() then hand the result to these functions, so the natural path is byte-for-byte identical in behaviour. forcePlayerRoll(faceNumber)/forceEnemyRoll(faceNumber) (new DEV ONLY section, explicitly commented for removal before any real release) look up the chosen face directly from gameState.die.faces / gameState.enemy.die.faces and call the exact same resolve*Roll() function — no bypass, no shortcut. Gating: refuses with [DEV] cannot force roll outside ROLL_PHASE / ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE when the phase doesn't match (silently returns with no log on a second attempt within the same phase visit, since that path isn't specified to log); a module-level playerForcedRollUsed/enemyForcedRollUsed flag is reset to false at the top of each ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE block (so exactly one forced roll is available per visit, on top of whatever the natural roll already did) and set true on first forced use. Forced rolls log [DEV] forced roll: face N before the normal [ROLL]/[ENEMY ROLL] lines that resolve*Roll() already produces. Wiring: renderDieList() takes a new optional forceRollFn parameter and attaches a DEV-ONLY-commented click listener per face button; refreshInspector()'s two renderDieList() calls now pass forcePlayerRoll/forceEnemyRoll respectively. Added a .face-btn:hover state (border/color/background lift, cursor:pointer) so the click targets are visually obvious, and a [DEV] log prefix (log-dev, bright cyan #22d3ee bold) so forced rolls are never mistaken for real ones in the log. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 019.
Stage 1.10 dev tool — load mod on demand (BUILD 020). Added a DEV ONLY control (#devModLoader: a mod <select>, a face-number <input>, Load/Clear buttons) placed next to the State Inspector in controls-row, not in the play area, with only enough inline CSS to lay it out — nothing else restyled. devLoadMod()/devClearFace() write through updateDie({faces: newFaces}) — never touch gameState.die directly — cloning the faces array and replacing one entry's modId (to 'consecrate' etc., or to null for clear); since resolvePlayerRoll() and the MOD_TRIGGER dispatcher only ever read face.modId, a runtime-loaded mod dispatches and registers listeners identically to one placed at init — no special-cased path. Both functions refuse faces 1 and 20 (NAT_ONE/NAT_TWENTY, "cannot change" per the die face law) with [DEV] cannot modify face 1 or face 20 (NAT faces). Loads log [DEV] loaded ModName onto face N, clears log [DEV] cleared face N. renderDevModOptions() populates the <select> from Object.keys(config.mods) once at the end of init() (not on every refreshInspector(), so the dropdown selection isn't reset by unrelated state updates). "No residual listeners from the mod that was there" is satisfied by the existing architecture, not new cleanup code: a mod's listeners are only ever registered when it's actually triggered (via resolvePlayerRoll's MOD_TRIGGER branch), never when merely loaded onto a face, and any turn-scoped listener a prior trigger did register is already removed by the existing clearListeners('turn') call in START_OF_TURN — clearing a face just stops future triggers by setting modId back to null, which the existing null-check in resolvePlayerRoll already routes to BLANK_ROLL with no extra branching needed. The existing force-roll tool and natural roll path were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 020.
Stage 1.10 dev mod loader readability fix (BUILD 021, CSS only, no logic touched — markup/JS re-verified unchanged after edit). #devModLoader bumped from 10px to 12px font with 5px/8px padding on its select/input/buttons (up from 2px/4px), gap 4px→8px, #devFaceInput widened 40px→52px to fit the larger digits — roughly matching the State Inspector toggle's generic 11px/4px-10px button sizing rather than the play-area's enlarged Start Game/Next Phase buttons, so it reads clearly without competing with them for attention. Added a dashed top border + extra top margin/padding to visually separate the dev strip from the State Inspector above it, reinforcing that it's a dev tool, not part of play. Game panels, hand row, and log were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 021.
Stage 1.10 blank passive value change (BUILD 022, config/constant change only — verified generateBlock(2) landed on onBlankRoll and nowhere else, Consecrate's own generateBlock(3) at line ~1219 confirmed untouched). onBlankRoll now calls generateBlock(2) instead of generateBlock(3); the BLANK_ROLL listener registration, MOD_TRIGGER routing, and mod dispatcher are all unchanged. Rationale (now recorded in SCOPE — V1 and CLASS OBJECT STRUCTURE too): 18 of 20 faces are blank, so the passive paid out on ~90% of turns, not 5% — 3 block against intent 10 negated 30% of incoming damage for free with no decision attached; 2 block is 20%, leaving room for block mods to matter. The Design Floor line was also corrected: it now compares mod value directly against 2 block on the triggering turn (target ~3–5× a blank, i.e. 6–12 points), explicitly noting that trigger frequency cancels out because a mod and a blank on the same face share the identical roll chance — the prior ×0.05-trigger-rate version of this floor was wrong and inflated the bar twentyfold; that arithmetic is now flagged as incorrect in CLAUDE.md rather than left as the standard. Consecrate, the dev tools, and the enemy were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 022.
Stage 1.10 dev tool bug fix — force roll stacking (BUILD 023). Root cause: runPhase()'s ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE blocks auto-resolved a natural roll the instant the phase was entered, so a subsequent forced click added a second roll's worth of effects on top instead of replacing anything. Fix: those blocks no longer auto-roll — entering ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE now only resets a shared playerRollResolved/enemyRollResolved flag to false. The natural roll is deferred to nextPhase(): when leaving ROLL_PHASE or ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE, if that flag is still false (nothing forced it), nextPhase() resolves it naturally (rollDie + resolvePlayerRoll/resolveEnemyRoll) before advancing; if a forced click already resolved it, this is skipped. forcePlayerRoll()/forceEnemyRoll() now read and set these same shared flags (renamed from the old playerForcedRollUsed/enemyForcedRollUsed, moved out of the DEV ONLY section since they're now core roll-state, not dev-only bookkeeping) instead of a separate dev-only flag, so natural and forced rolls compete for the same single-resolution slot — whichever happens first (a forced face click, or leaving the phase via Next Phase) is the turn's one roll, and the other path is a no-op. Existing rules preserved: forced rolls still refuse outside the relevant phase with [DEV] cannot force roll outside ROLL_PHASE / ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and still log [DEV] forced roll: face N. Mod effects, the blank passive value, and the load-mod tool were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 023.
Stage 1.10 first pool mod — Smite (BUILD 024). Added config.mods['smite']: effect() runs calculateDamage(16) (per Law 1), applies it via updateEnemy({hp: ...}), fires ON_DAMAGE_DEALT with source 'smite', and logs [MOD] smite: N damage (N is the live calculateDamage() result, not a hardcoded 16, matching how Consecrate logs its own dynamic amounts). No conditions, no scaling, no persistence — a single effect() call, no registerListener(), unlike Consecrate. Dispatches through the existing generic MOD_TRIGGER listener exactly like every other mod; no new phase logic, no changes to resolvePlayerRoll/runPhase/nextPhase. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed only appears in the config.mods definition and its own internal source string) — loadable via the existing dev load-mod dropdown, which already picks it up automatically since renderDevModOptions() reads Object.keys(config.mods). Enemy HP flash-on-change feedback (from the BUILD 016 UI pass) needed no new wiring — it already diffs gameState.enemy.hp on every renderStats() call regardless of source. Consecrate, the blank passive, and the dev tools were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 024.
Stage 1.10 fight-length balance pass (BUILD 025, four targeted number changes, no other logic touched — re-verified by reading each edited line plus Ward/Consecrate/Smite unchanged). gameState.enemy.hp/maxHp 45→70. Strike's calculateDamage(6)→calculateDamage(5). Rite's damage calculateDamage(6)→calculateDamage(5); Rite's own generateBlock(6) confirmed untouched. CARD_EFFECT_TEXT's display strings updated to match (strike "5 damage", rite "5 damage 6 block") so the hand-card UI stays truthful. Ward (generateBlock(5)), soul per turn, hand size, deck size, enemy intent (10), the blank passive (generateBlock(2)), Consecrate, and Smite were all confirmed unchanged by direct re-read. Recorded in CLAUDE.md: enemy HP is the axis carrying progression across a run, not mod numbers — mod values stay single/low-double-digit and multipliers stay modest while enemy HP scales instead. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 025.
Stage 1.10 variable enemy intent (BUILD 026, one addition — re-verified by reading the full START_OF_TURN block and grepping every remaining "intent" reference in the file). START_OF_TURN now rolls a fresh intent (Math.floor(Math.random()*13)+6, i.e. 6–18 inclusive) right after the round increment, applies it via updateEnemy({intent}), and logs [ENEMY] intent set to N — before poison/block/soul/draw, so it's always decided well before CARD_PHASE. No other code needed to change: ENEMY_ACT_PHASE already read gameState.enemy.intent live (unchanged), and renderStats() already wrote both #enemyIntentValue and #enemyIntentLabel ("Attacks for N") from gameState.enemy.intent on every render — since updateEnemy() already triggers refreshInspector() internally, the display updates automatically the instant the new intent is rolled, with no stale-value window. Enemy HP, player HP, soul, the blank passive, Consecrate, Smite, and all card values were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 026.
Stage 1.10 third pool mod — Penance (BUILD 027). Added config.mods['penance']: calculateDamage(8) applied via updateEnemy (Law 1), generateBlock(8) applied via updatePlayer (same path the blank passive and Consecrate already use), both fire their existing ON_DAMAGE_DEALT/ON_BLOCK_GENERATED hooks, and it logs [MOD] penance: N damage, N block with live computed amounts. No conditions, no scaling, no persistence — a single effect() call, no registerListener(), dispatches through the existing generic MOD_TRIGGER listener exactly like Smite and Consecrate; no phase logic touched. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed, and the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — loadable via the existing dev tool, auto-picked-up since renderDevModOptions() reads Object.keys(config.mods) generically (confirmed unchanged). Visual feedback: unlike Smite/Consecrate (deliberately left untouched, no shared/generic mod-fx mechanism existed before this), Penance's effect() calls the existing flashElement() helper directly for both halves — enemy panel shake + enemy HP number pop, player panel pulse + player Block number pop — matching what card-played Rite already triggers via the separate CARD_FX_TYPE path. This is self-contained inside Penance's own definition; nothing shared (log(), CARD_FX_TYPE, triggerCardFx) was touched, so Smite and Consecrate's feedback behaviour is unchanged. Enemy intent and the dev tools were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 027.
Stage 1.10 fourth pool mod — Offering (BUILD 028). Added config.mods['offering']: updatePlayer soul +2, logs [MOD] offering: +2 soul this turn, draw 1, then calls the existing drawCards(1) unchanged (grep-confirmed drawCards() itself untouched, reshuffle-on-empty-deck behaviour still intact). Both the soul and the draw are usable before CARD_PHASE for the same structural reason Consecrate's soul grant already is — MOD_TRIGGER fires during ROLL_PHASE, which the fixed PHASE_ORDER always places before CARD_PHASE — no new scheduling logic was needed. No turn-scoped listener was registered: unlike Consecrate's block-per-card effect (which needs one because it must react to future ON_CARD_PLAY events this turn), Offering's soul grant is a single immediate update with nothing to react to later, and it already can't persist because START_OF_TURN unconditionally resets soul to maxSoul every turn regardless of what it was — clearListeners('turn') has nothing to do here since nothing was registered. The drawn card needs no special handling either — it's a normal card.id in hand, discarded at END_PLAYER_TURN like any other. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — auto-loadable via the dev tool as before. Smite, Penance, Consecrate, the blank passive, enemy intent, and the dev tools were not touched. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 028.
Stage 1.10 turn-flow auto-advance (BUILD 029, control-flow/UI only — no mod, card, intent, or blank-passive logic touched, re-verified by direct re-read). Added autoAdvance(): a do-while wrapper around the existing (unrenamed) nextPhase() that keeps single-stepping until gameState.turn.phase === 'CARD_PHASE' or gameState.run.status !== 'active' — the do-while guarantees at least one step, so calling it from CARD_PHASE (End Turn) correctly runs a full turn instead of a no-op. runPhase()'s existing top-of-function win/loss guard was not touched and still halts the chain correctly mid-loop (phase never reaches CARD_PHASE, but the while condition's status check exits the loop). Added resetFight() per the FIGHT RESET spec: player block/soul/deck(reshuffled)/hand/discard/poison reset, hp carries over; enemy hp/poison/buffs reset; turn round/cardsPlayedThisTurn reset to 0; clearListeners('fight'); run.status back to 'active'; die weights/mods untouched. Buttons: #startGameBtn relabelled "Restart Fight", now calls resetFight() + runPhase('START_OF_TURN') + autoAdvance(). The old #nextPhaseBtn was replaced in the play area by #endTurnBtn ("End Turn", styled with the same large game-font rule startGameBtn already had), which just calls autoAdvance() and is disabled unless run.status==='active' && phase==='CARD_PHASE'. The old single-step behaviour was preserved verbatim and relocated to a new #devStepControl block next to #devModLoader at the bottom of the page (id renamed devNextPhaseBtn, DEV ONLY comments added, disabled whenever run.status!=='active', same as before under its old name) — grep-confirmed zero remaining references to the old nextPhaseBtn id anywhere. init() now ends by calling runPhase('START_OF_TURN') then autoAdvance(), so the page loads straight into round 1 CARD_PHASE with a hand drawn and a roll resolved, with no button click required. Every phase runPhase() enters during an auto-advanced chain still logs its own [PHASE] line exactly as before — nothing in runPhase()'s logging was touched, so the log stays a complete record. Force roll (forcePlayerRoll/forceEnemyRoll) is unmodified; it remains usable exactly as before whenever the game is actually sitting in ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE — reachable during normal play via the auto-chain's own natural roll (which the forced path still races against and replaces, unchanged), or deliberately via the relocated dev step control single-stepping to that phase and pausing there before the tester forces a face. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 029.
Stage 1.10 enemy poison tick (BUILD 030). START_OF_TURN's phase-machine block gained an enemy poison branch (immediately after the existing player-poison branch): if gameState.enemy.poisonStacks > 0, damage = calculateDamage(poisonStacks) per Law 1, applied to enemy.hp via updateEnemy alongside poisonStacks - 1 in the same call, logged as [POISON] N damage, stacks now M; at 0 stacks it logs nothing and does nothing. Poison bypasses block by construction (applied straight to hp, never routed through ENEMY_ACT_PHASE's intent-minus-block subtraction), with an explicit comment at the call site for when enemy block exists later. Persistence and reset needed no new code: poisonStacks already lived in gameState.enemy and resetFight() already zeroed it. The Poison Stacks stat panel (#enemyPoisonValue) already read gameState.enemy.poisonStacks live from a prior stage, so it started reflecting real values automatically. Added a DEV ONLY control (#devPoisonApplier: number input + "Apply to Enemy" button) beside the existing DEV LOAD MOD bar, wired to devApplyPoison(), which adds the entered amount via updateEnemy and logs [DEV] applied N poison to enemy — no mod or card applies poison yet. No card, mod, Consecrate/Smite/Penance/Offering, blank passive, enemy intent, or turn-flow logic was touched. Verified via a full jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: dev-applying 6 poison set gameState.enemy.poisonStacks to 6 and the panel read 6; six consecutive End Turn clicks produced enemy HP drops of exactly 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1 (21 total) with stacks reading 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0; a seventh End Turn produced no [POISON] line; Restart Fight after re-applying 9 poison brought poisonStacks back to 0; zero JS errors captured. Still needs a manual hard-refresh browser pass to close out the substage. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 030.
Stage 1.10 fifth and sixth pool mods — Blight and Virulence (BUILD 031). Both added to config.mods immediately after Offering, using only the existing gameState.enemy.poisonStacks field — the same one the dev poison applier writes to and the START_OF_TURN decay tick reads from — so no phase logic and no changes to the decay tick were needed. Blight: effect() sets poisonStacks to current + 6 via updateEnemy, logs [MOD] blight: applied 6 poison. Virulence: effect() computes afterApply = current + 3, doubled = afterApply * 2, sets poisonStacks to doubled via updateEnemy, logs [MOD] virulence: applied 3 poison, doubled to N (N is the live computed value, not hardcoded). Neither registers a listener — single effect() calls like Smite/Penance/Offering, not Consecrate. Neither is placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — both auto-loadable via the existing dev load-mod dropdown since renderDevModOptions() reads Object.keys(config.mods) generically. Poison Stacks panel needed no new wiring — updateEnemy already triggers refreshInspector() -> renderStats() -> reads gameState.enemy.poisonStacks live, so it updates immediately on either mod's trigger. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across three scenarios: (1) load Blight face 6, force roll — log reads [MOD] blight: applied 6 poison, poisonStacks and panel both read 6, and the existing decay tick still fires normally afterward (stacks 6→5, enemy HP dropped exactly 6 on the next full turn cycle); (2) fresh fight, load Virulence face 6, force roll — log reads [MOD] virulence: applied 3 poison, doubled to 6, poisonStacks and panel both read 6; (3) fresh fight, Blight on face 6 and Virulence on face 7, force roll Blight then let one decay tick pass then force roll Virulence on a later turn — Virulence's doubled value matched (remaining stacks + 3) * 2 exactly against whatever the decay had left, confirming doubling correctly compounds on top of decay remainder rather than a fixed baseline. Zero JS errors across all three runs. Consecrate, Smite, Penance, Offering, the poison decay tick, the blank passive, enemy intent, and the dev tools were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html sections after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 031.
Stage 1.10 force-roll auto-advance pause fix (BUILD 032). Root cause: BUILD 029's autoAdvance() was a fully synchronous do-while loop, so once it entered ROLL_PHASE it called nextPhase() (resolving the natural roll and advancing to CARD_PHASE) in the very same tick, with no gap in the JS event loop for a face-button click to ever be processed — force roll was reachable only via the DEV STEP fallback, which manually pauses between every phase since it never calls autoAdvance() at all. Fix: autoAdvance() is now the entry point into autoAdvanceStep(), which behaves identically to the old synchronous loop for every phase except ROLL_PHASE — when gameState.turn.phase === 'ROLL_PHASE' and playerRollResolved is still false, it defers via setTimeout(ROLL_PHASE_PAUSE_MS = 300) instead of calling nextPhase() immediately, giving the browser event loop a real window to run a forcePlayerRoll() click handler first. When the timeout fires, it calls the same nextPhase() used everywhere else, whose existing !playerRollResolved guard (unchanged) is what actually prevents a double roll: if a forced click already resolved it during the pause, nextPhase() skips the natural roll and just advances the phase; if nothing forced it, nextPhase() resolves the natural roll exactly as before. A stale-continuation guard (if the phase is no longer ROLL_PHASE when the timeout fires, abandon this continuation) covers the edge case of Restart Fight being clicked mid-pause, which starts its own independent autoAdvance chain. continueAutoAdvance() reproduces the old do-while's stop condition unchanged: keep stepping while run.status is 'active' and phase isn't CARD_PHASE. nextPhase(), runPhase(), PHASE_ORDER, DEV STEP's direct nextPhase() call, forcePlayerRoll()/forceEnemyRoll(), and every mod (Blight/Virulence included) were untouched. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: (1) with no force-roll click, waiting past the 300ms pause after page load lands in CARD_PHASE with a 5-card hand, same as before; (2) loading Blight onto face 6 and clicking its force-roll button 50ms into the ROLL_PHASE pause resolved exactly one [ROLL] log line (the forced one, blight applying 6 poison) with no natural roll underneath it, then the chain continued on to CARD_PHASE on its own; (3) DEV STEP still advances exactly one named phase per click (CARD_PHASE -> END_PLAYER_TURN -> ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE, one click each); (4) End Turn from CARD_PHASE still lands in CARD_PHASE of the next round with round incremented by exactly 1, just carrying the same brief pause at the intervening ROLL_PHASE. Zero JS errors across all four checks. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 032.
Stage 1.10 force-roll pause length fix (BUILD 033). BUILD 032's mechanism (defer via setTimeout, let the existing playerRollResolved guard in nextPhase() prevent a double roll) was already structurally correct — playtesting showed the failure was purely that 300ms is not enough time for a human to visually locate and click one specific face among twenty, so clicks kept landing after auto-advance had already moved on to CARD_PHASE and were correctly refused with [DEV] cannot force roll outside ROLL_PHASE. Fix is a single constant change: ROLL_PHASE_PAUSE_MS 300 → 1800, turning the ROLL_PHASE pause inside autoAdvanceStep() into a genuine ~1.8s deliberate wait instead of a sub-300ms race window — no other line in autoAdvanceStep()/continueAutoAdvance()/nextPhase() changed. Comments at the constant and around autoAdvanceStep() were expanded to record why 300ms failed and to spell out explicitly (per this prompt's requirements) that: only a face click (via forcePlayerRoll's existing phase gate) or the dev tools can interrupt the wait, since End Turn and every other control are already disabled outside CARD_PHASE via refreshInspector()'s existing endTurnBtn.disabled line (no new gating needed); and the pause is scoped only to the player's ROLL_PHASE branch inside autoAdvanceStep() — ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE and every other phase transition are a different code path entirely and were not touched. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: (1) 1000ms after entering ROLL_PHASE (well past the old broken 300ms window), phase was still ROLL_PHASE and playerRollResolved still false, proving this is a real ~1.8s wait and not a race; (2) loading Blight onto face 6 and clicking its force-roll button 1000ms into the pause (the exact scenario that failed under BUILD 032) forced correctly with no "cannot force roll" refusal, exactly one [ROLL] log line total, and blight's 6 poison applied; (3) with no click at all, waiting out the full ~1.8s pause resolved the natural roll and landed in CARD_PHASE with a 5-card hand and zero errors, same as always; (4) isolated via DEV STEP single-stepping, the ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE -> ENEMY_ACT_PHASE transition took 4ms, confirming the enemy roll and every other phase transition remain completely unaffected by the player-roll pause. Zero JS errors across all checks. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 033.
Stage 1.10 seventh pool mod — Sanctuary (BUILD 034). Added config.mods['sanctuary'] immediately after Virulence: effect() runs generateBlock(16) (Law 1's block equivalent) and applies it via updatePlayer({ block: gameState.player.block + block }) — the same path the blank passive, Ward, Rite, and Penance already use — fires the existing ON_BLOCK_GENERATED hook with source 'sanctuary', and logs [MOD] sanctuary: N block (N is the live generateBlock() result, not hardcoded, matching how every other mod logs its computed amount). No conditions, no scaling, no persistence — a single effect() call, no registerListener(), dispatching through the existing generic MOD_TRIGGER listener exactly like Smite/Penance/Offering/Blight/Virulence. Visual feedback reuses the exact block-only pair Ward's card play already triggers (flashElement 'playerPanel' fx-pulse-blue, flashElement 'playerBlockValue' fx-pop-blue) rather than Penance's dual attack+block feedback, since Sanctuary is block-only. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — auto-loadable via the existing dev load-mod dropdown since renderDevModOptions() reads Object.keys(config.mods) generically. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: loading Sanctuary onto face 6 and force-rolling it during the ROLL_PHASE pause logged exactly [MOD] sanctuary: 16 block with player block going 0→16, both fx-pop-blue (on the Block number) and fx-pulse-blue (on the player panel) classes present immediately after; advancing to the next round's START_OF_TURN cleared block back to 0 as normal; clearing face 6 and force-rolling it again returned to the blank passive's 2 block with zero residual Sanctuary behaviour. Zero JS errors. Consecrate, Smite, Penance, Offering, Blight, Virulence, the poison decay tick, the blank passive, enemy intent, the force-roll pause timing, and the dev tools were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html sections after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 034.
Stage 1.10 eighth pool mod — Vigil (BUILD 035). Added config.mods['vigil'] immediately after Sanctuary. Unlike every other mod so far, its payout must read hand size at END_PLAYER_TURN (after the player has played cards) rather than at roll time, since ROLL_PHASE always precedes CARD_PHASE and hand size there is always the fixed post-draw 5 — reading it then would make the mod a flat number in a costume. effect() itself only logs the trigger ([MOD] vigil: registered, block scales with cards held at end of turn) and registers a turn-scoped listener via registerListener('END_PLAYER_TURN', 'vigil_block_per_card_held', fn, 'turn') — the exact same clearOn:'turn' pattern Consecrate's own block-per-card listener already uses on ON_CARD_PLAY, so it is guaranteed to clear at the next START_OF_TURN's existing clearListeners('turn') call and cannot persist past the turn it triggered on or double-fire. The listener itself reads gameState.player.hand.length at call time, computes generateBlock(5 * cardsHeld) (Law 1's block equivalent, so any block multipliers/flat additions still apply), applies it via updatePlayer({ block: gameState.player.block + block }) — the same accumulation path every other block source uses — fires ON_BLOCK_GENERATED with source 'vigil', and logs [MOD] vigil: N cards held, granted 5xN block (N is the live hand.length, "5xN" is literal notation per the prompt's exact spec, not the computed total). No new phase logic was needed: runPhase()'s existing callListeners(phase) call already fires before END_PLAYER_TURN's own hand-discard block runs (confirmed by re-reading runPhase() — callListeners(phase) is unconditionally the first thing every phase does, discard happens afterward inside the phase === 'END_PLAYER_TURN' branch), so Vigil's listener always sees the hand exactly as the player left it, never the post-discard empty array. Because the resulting block lands via the same gameState.player.block field as everything else, it needed no special handling to survive into ENEMY_ACT_PHASE (which already reads gameState.player.block live) or to clear at the next START_OF_TURN (which already unconditionally zeroes block). Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — auto-loadable via the existing dev load-mod dropdown. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: loading Vigil, force-rolling it, then playing cards down to 3 held and ending turn logged exactly [MOD] vigil: 3 cards held, granted 5x3 block, block went from 11 to 26 before ENEMY_ACT_PHASE, and the enemy's attack that turn (intent 11) was logged fully blocked against that 26 — confirming the vigil block was live and counted at damage-calc time; the following turn (not re-rolled) produced no [MOD] vigil line at all, confirming the turn-scoped listener does not persist or re-fire; forcing hand to 0 (the 3-soul-per-turn economy makes literally emptying a 5-card hand via card play mathematically impossible — min cost 1 soul × 3 soul = at most 3 plays, always leaving >=2 — so the true zero-hand state was set directly via the same updatePlayer() helper END_PLAYER_TURN's own discard step already uses) produced exactly [MOD] vigil: 0 cards held, granted 5x0 block with no errors. Zero JS errors across both scenarios. Consecrate, Sanctuary, Smite, Penance, Offering, Blight, Virulence, the poison decay tick, the blank passive, enemy intent, the force-roll pause timing, and the dev tools were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html sections after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 035.
Stage 1.10 Restart Fight dev-loading pause (BUILD 036). #startGameBtn's click handler no longer calls autoAdvance() after resetFight() + runPhase('START_OF_TURN') — it now stops there deliberately, sitting in START_OF_TURN with the hand already drawn (drawCards(5) is part of START_OF_TURN's own runPhase() logic, unaffected), giving unlimited time to load mods via the existing dev load-mod tool before the first roll of a fresh fight. init()'s own end-of-load call (runPhase('START_OF_TURN'); autoAdvance();) was left untouched — this change is scoped to the Restart Fight button only, per the prompt. Added a new DEV ONLY #devBeginRollBtn ("Begin Roll") next to #devNextPhaseBtn inside the existing #devStepControl block, rather than repurposing DEV STEP itself: DEV STEP's handler calls nextPhase() directly, which would move the phase to ROLL_PHASE but never arm the ROLL_PHASE force-roll pause (that pause lives only inside autoAdvanceStep()/continueAutoAdvance()), so it would leave the game sitting in ROLL_PHASE with no auto-resolve ever scheduled — not "the existing pause working normally." Begin Roll's handler instead calls autoAdvance() (the same call the old Restart Fight handler used to make directly), so it goes through the exact same autoAdvanceStep() path that both End Turn and every natural turn use: since the phase entering isn't yet ROLL_PHASE, it takes nextPhase() once (START_OF_TURN -> ROLL_PHASE), then continueAutoAdvance() finds it in ROLL_PHASE and arms the standard ROLL_PHASE_PAUSE_MS setTimeout exactly as normal. Gated via a new refreshInspector() line (document.getElementById('devBeginRollBtn').disabled = !(run.status==='active' && turn.phase==='START_OF_TURN')), mirroring the existing endTurnBtn/devNextPhaseBtn disabled pattern, so it's only actionable in the one moment it's meant for. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: after Restart Fight, phase was START_OF_TURN with a 5-card hand and stayed there through a full 2.5s wait with no auto-advance (proving no pause was ever armed); two mods (Sanctuary, Vigil) were loaded onto two faces during that window; clicking Begin Roll moved phase to ROLL_PHASE immediately and re-disabled the button; 1000ms later phase was still ROLL_PHASE (pause genuinely armed), and force-rolling a face there resolved correctly ([MOD] sanctuary: 16 block, +16 player block) with the normal pause mechanics; after that turn, End Turn was clicked and the following turn auto-advanced to CARD_PHASE exactly as before with round incremented by 1. Zero JS errors. End Turn's own handler, nextPhase(), runPhase(), the ROLL_PHASE pause constant/logic, and every mod were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html sections after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 036.
Stage 1.10 init() dev-loading pause fix (BUILD 037). BUILD 036 only paused the sequence after Restart Fight; init() still ended with its own separate runPhase('START_OF_TURN'); autoAdvance(); call, so a hard refresh's very first fight of a session still rolled immediately with no setup window — the exact same bug on a second entry point. Fix unifies both call sites onto one new shared function, startFreshTurnPaused() (defined next to resetFight(), just above the Restart Fight click handler): it does nothing but runPhase('START_OF_TURN') — deliberately no autoAdvance() call — so it always stops in START_OF_TURN with the hand already drawn, regardless of who calls it. init()'s old runPhase('START_OF_TURN'); autoAdvance(); pair was replaced with a single startFreshTurnPaused() call; the Restart Fight click handler's runPhase('START_OF_TURN') was replaced with the same startFreshTurnPaused() call (grep-confirmed: exactly one function definition, exactly two call sites — init() and startGameBtn's handler — and zero remaining standalone runPhase('START_OF_TURN') calls anywhere else in the file). No second proceed control was added — #devBeginRollBtn from BUILD 036 is reused unchanged for both entry points, since both now leave the game in the identical START_OF_TURN state it already expects, and its existing refreshInspector() disabled-gating (turn.phase === 'START_OF_TURN') needed no changes to cover the init() path. Function-declaration hoisting makes the forward reference from init() (which appears earlier in the file) to startFreshTurnPaused() (defined later, near resetFight()) safe, since init() itself only actually runs on the DOMContentLoaded listener at the very bottom of the script, well after the whole file has parsed. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html, simulating a hard refresh (not Restart Fight): on load, phase was START_OF_TURN with a 5-card hand and stayed there through a full 2.5s wait with no auto-advance; two mods (Sanctuary, Vigil) were loaded during that window; clicking Begin Roll moved phase to ROLL_PHASE, stayed there 1000ms into the pause (armed correctly), and force-rolling a face resolved correctly ([MOD] sanctuary: 16 block, +16 player block); Restart Fight was then clicked again mid-session and reproduced the identical pause-then-Begin-Roll-then-normal-resolve sequence via the same shared function. Zero JS errors. End Turn, DEV STEP, nextPhase(), runPhase(), the ROLL_PHASE pause constant/logic, resetFight(), and every mod were not touched — re-verified by re-reading the modified index.html sections after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 037.
Stage 1.10 post-fight die action loop — Load/Strengthen/Skip (BUILD 038). The nucleus feature: victory no longer just halts, it now offers exactly one player-facing die action before a new fight can begin, using the existing mod pool only (no new mods, no map/run structure, no card rewards — explicitly out of scope this substage). New module-level UI-flow state (dieActionStep, dieActionMods, dieActionChosenModId — same non-gameState convention playerRollResolved/lastEnemyHp already use, since this drives which panel renders rather than affecting win/loss or damage) tracks a small state machine: null (inactive) -> 'choose' (Load/Strengthen/Skip) -> either 'load_pick_mod' -> 'load_pick_face' or 'strengthen_pick_face' -> back to null once resolved. openDieActionScreen()/closeDieActionScreen() set this and call refreshInspector(); the actual choice functions (dieActionChooseLoad/Strengthen/Skip, dieActionPickMod, dieActionPickLoadFace, dieActionPickStrengthenFace) live alongside them. Load draws exactly 3 mod ids via Object.keys(config.mods) filtered against DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS = ['consecrate','zeal','fervour','ordain'] (Consecrate excluded as the class anchor per SCOPE — V1; Zeal/Fervour/Ordain excluded by id now even though they don't exist yet, so they're auto-excluded once built without touching this code again) and against every modId currently loaded on gameState.die.faces, then shuffle().slice(0,3) — reusing the existing shuffle() helper, no new randomness code. Picking a mod moves to face selection restricted to blank faces (modId === null); picking a face writes through updateDie() (cloning the faces array and replacing one entry, the same pattern devLoadMod() already uses) and logs [DIE ACTION] loaded ModName onto face N. Strengthen skips straight to face selection restricted to currently loaded faces excluding face 1/20 (NAT_ONE/NAT_TWENTY are not "loaded" mod faces per the die face law, so strengthening them was deliberately excluded even though the prompt didn't explicitly say so — matches how Load already can't touch those faces), writes the same face's weight + 1 through updateDie(), and logs [DIE ACTION] strengthened face N to weight W. Skip just logs [DIE ACTION] skipped and closes with no state change. New renderDieActionPanel() (called every refreshInspector(), same as every other render* function) builds the panel's buttons fresh from the current step; a new #dieActionPanel <div> (hidden by default, shown/populated only while a step is active) sits between the hand row and the controls row, with its own minimal CSS (gold-bordered panel, wrapping button row) — this is genuinely player-facing UI, not a dev tool, per the prompt. The auto-trigger: runPhase()'s existing top-of-function win guard now calls openDieActionScreen() immediately after logging [WIN] enemy defeated (one line added, nothing else in that guard touched). A new DEV ONLY #devSkipToDieActionBtn ("Skip to Die Action") sits in the existing #devStepControl block and just calls openDieActionScreen() directly without touching run.status — same function the real win trigger calls, so it exercises the real flow rather than a parallel copy, exactly as the prompt asked. Gating: #startGameBtn.disabled = (dieActionStep !== null) (new line) prevents starting a new fight mid-choice; #endTurnBtn's existing disabled condition gained `&& dieActionStep === null` (so the dev shortcut can't be used to open the panel over an in-progress CARD_PHASE and still let End Turn fire underneath it) — no other condition on that line changed. Requirement four (die state persisting across the reset) needed no code change at all: resetFight() already never touched gameState.die (confirmed by re-reading it — only player/enemy/turn/registry/run are touched), matching the FIGHT RESET spec's "Die: weights and mods unchanged, persist between fights" line that predates this substage; Restart Fight already goes through the shared startFreshTurnPaused() from BUILD 037 afterward. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html across two scenarios: (1) using the dev shortcut, all three paths were exercised back-to-back on one die — Skip left the die byte-for-byte unchanged and logged [DIE ACTION] skipped; Strengthen listed only the one currently-loaded face (10, Consecrate) and correctly bumped its weight 1→2 with the matching log line; Load then offered exactly 3 mods (all correctly excluding consecrate/zeal/fervour/ordain and, on the later pass, smite once it was loaded), picking one and a blank face correctly wrote that mod onto the chosen face with the matching log line; (2) a real fight was played to an actual win (dev-loading Smite and force-rolling it across several turns until enemy HP crossed 0), the screen appeared automatically with dieActionStep 'choose' and the panel visibly flex-displayed with zero manual intervention, Load correctly excluded the already-loaded Smite from its 3 offered mods, the picked mod was loaded onto a chosen blank face, and clicking Restart Fight afterward reset enemy HP to 70/70, player hand to a fresh 5-card draw (deck+hand+discard totalling 10), and player HP correctly carried over per the FIGHT RESET spec — while both the pre-existing Smite face and the newly loaded reward face were still present on the die, confirmed unchanged. Zero JS errors across both scenarios. Consecrate, Sanctuary, Smite, Penance, Offering, Blight, Virulence, Vigil, enemy stats, card values, and every existing dev tool besides the one new shortcut button were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 038.
Dev tooling addition, not a new substage — DEV POISON target selector (BUILD 039). #devPoisonApplier gained a <select id="devPoisonTargetSelect"> (Enemy/Player, defaulting to Enemy) between the DEV POISON label and the amount input, all three new lines marked DEV ONLY inline matching the existing comment style. devApplyPoison() now branches on the select's value: Player routes through updatePlayer({ poisonStacks: gameState.player.poisonStacks + amount }) — the same helper every other player mutation already uses, no direct gameState.player write — and logs [DEV] applied N poison to player; Enemy path is untouched, still updateEnemy() with the same log text as BUILD 038, confirmed byte-for-byte identical by direct re-read. No new UI element was needed for visibility: the player stat panel's existing "Active debuffs" row (#playerDebuffsValue) already rendered gameState.player.poisonStacks live (poison xN or —) since a prior stage, so it started reflecting dev-applied player poison automatically. The player poison tick itself (runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN branch, unmodified per the prompt) was exercised in a browser-equivalent environment for the first time via this tool, and its behaviour was found to diverge from the intended decay model: verified via jsdom by calling runPhase('START_OF_TURN') directly five times in a row with poisonStacks pinned at 6 and HP reset to 70 before each call — every single call dealt exactly 6 damage and poisonStacks stayed at 6 after all five calls (stacksBefore/stacksAfter identical every time). The tick's own code only ever computes poisonDamage from the current stack count and reduces player.hp — it contains no poisonStacks decrement at all, unlike the enemy poison tick (BUILD 030) which explicitly writes poisonStacks: newStacks (current - 1) alongside the damage in the same updateEnemy() call. Net effect: player poison is currently a flat, permanent N-damage-per-turn source, not a decaying 6/5/4/3/2/1 stack — confirmed, not fixed, per the prompt's explicit instruction to change nothing and report the difference. Block interaction requirement holds by construction regardless of the decay question: the tick's code path never reads or writes gameState.player.block in any way, so block is neither reduced nor able to absorb any of the damage. Restart Fight still clears player poison to 0 (resetFight() already zeroed poisonStacks unconditionally, independent of whatever the tick does). Zero JS errors. calculateDamage(), the damage pipeline, the phase machine's other branches, the mod pool, the die action panel, and the enemy poison path were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 039.
Bug fix, not a new substage — .combat-panel align-items: flex-start (BUILD 040). PARTIAL FIX — see below. Follows a two-step investigation: an initial attempt to add overflow: hidden to #playerPanel (or .stats-col) to stop hand-card-row bleed-through was aborted after measuring the real rendered layout in headless Edge (via Playwright driving the system msedge.exe against the actual index.html — jsdom cannot compute real flex geometry) and finding hero-stats and even the Active debuffs row itself would have been clipped, not just excess below them, at every tested viewport including 1080p. Root cause identified: .combat-panel used the flex default align-items: stretch, forcing .stats-col to match .die-col's compressed height even though .stats-col's own content (hero-stats + 3 stat-line rows, ~375-420px) cannot compress the way .die-col's 20 min-height:0 die-rows can. Applied fix: added align-items: flex-start to the existing .combat-panel rule (one line, with an explanatory comment) — no min-height, flex, font-size, or padding changed on any panel/column/stat-row, .die-col/.log/combat-panel structure otherwise untouched. Re-measured via the same real-browser harness: hero-stats now renders at its full undistorted 255px height (previously collapsed as low as 0px), and .stats-col now sizes to its true ~418px content height instead of being force-stretched to match .die-col. Confirmed via ancestor-chain inspection that no element between #playerPanel and the document root has overflow: hidden, so .fx-pulse-blue's box-shadow glow (Ward/Sanctuary/Consecrate/Penance/Rite block feedback) remains unclipped. .left-col was confirmed to scroll cleanly rather than clip when content genuinely exceeds its box (e.g. at 1600x700, left-col's scrollHeight (715) now exceeds its clientHeight (670) and it scrolls, where before the same shortfall silently bled instead). HOWEVER: real-browser screenshots at both required success viewports (1400x900 and 1600x1080) still show visible bleed-through into #handRow — the Soul stat icon/value and, at 1080p, the Active debuffs row are still visibly overlapping the hand-card row. Root cause of the remaining overlap: align-items on .combat-panel only governs how .die-col and .stats-col align relative to each other inside .combat-panel's own row layout; it does not and cannot change #playerPanel/.combat-panel's own outer box height, which is still governed by the unchanged flex: 1; min-height: 0 rule inherited from .combat-panel in the outer .left-col column context (confirmed identical outer height — 133px at 1400x900, 223px at 1600x1080 — before and after this change, via direct measurement). Since instructions explicitly forbid changing min-height/flex on any panel, this fix could not and did not touch the actual constraint producing #playerPanel's outer shortfall — it only fixed the internal distortion and stopped Deck/Discard specifically from overlapping (confirmed via bounding-box comparison, both cleared #handRow's range at every tested height after the fix, where before the fix Deck/Discard could also overlap). Hero-stats (and Active debuffs at taller viewports) still overlap #handRow because they render past #playerPanel's still-short outer box exactly as before, just without the distortion. This does not meet the stated success criterion "no player-panel content overlaps or bleeds into #handRow" — reported plainly rather than claimed complete. Build stamp updated to STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 040 as instructed, but the underlying visual bug is only partially resolved; a further fix touching the outer .left-col/#playerPanel flex distribution (which was out of scope for this ticket) is still needed.
Bug fix, not a new substage — .combat-panel min-height (BUILD 041). RESOLVES the bleed-through left open by BUILD 040. .combat-panel's min-height: 0 (the actual constraint producing #playerPanel's too-short outer box, confirmed as the root cause in BUILD 041's own investigation) was replaced with min-height: 512px, with a comment explaining the number's origin. The value was not guessed: measured via the same real-browser harness (Playwright driving the system's actual msedge.exe against the real index.html, not jsdom) by temporarily overriding .combat-panel with min-height: 2000px !important via an injected stylesheet (measurement only, never written to the file) and reading die-col's and stats-col's true unconstrained content heights for both panels — die-col (20 natural-height die-rows) needed 466px, taller than either stats-col (player 418px, enemy 357px), plus the rule's own 40px top/bottom padding and border, giving 508px measured minimum; 512px was chosen as a clean round number with a small buffer. Applied to the single shared .combat-panel rule (not a per-panel override), so both enemy and player panels get the same headroom — .die-col's own CSS (flex: 1.1; min-width: 0, and each .die-row's own min-height: 0) was not touched, it simply no longer needs to compress below natural size at normal viewports since its container now has room. Re-measured via the same real-browser method used in BUILD 040: at both required viewports (1400x900 and 1600x1080), #playerPanel and #enemyPanel both render at exactly 512px, hero-stats at its full undistorted 255px, and heroStats/debuffsRow/deckRow/discardRow all now test false for overlapping #handRow's bounding box (previously heroStats and, at 1080p, debuffsRow overlapped) — confirmed both by bounding-box comparison and by direct screenshots of the hand-row region at both viewports, showing Active debuffs/Deck/Discard fully legible above a clean gap with no text visible between or behind the hand cards. Ancestor-chain check repeated: no element between #playerPanel and the document root has overflow: hidden, so .fx-pulse-blue's glow (Ward/Sanctuary/Consecrate/Penance/Rite feedback) remains unclipped, confirmed unchanged from BUILD 040. .left-col now genuinely needs to scroll at every tested height (scrollHeight 1628 vs clientHeight 670-1050, all confirmed scrollable rather than clipping) since both panels no longer compress below their natural size — this is the expected, correct tradeoff named explicitly in the prompt. #log, .fx-pulse-blue's own rule, die-face row structure, and .die-col were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 041. The player-panel bleed-through bug opened by the original BUILD 038/039 layout and investigated across BUILD 040-041 is now closed.
Bug fix, not a new substage — player poison decay + calculateDamage() routing (BUILD 042). Fixes the divergence confirmed by direct investigation (six consecutive START_OF_TURN ticks all dealt a flat 6 damage with poisonStacks never decrementing) and completes Law 1 compliance for the player poison path in the same edit, since both live in the same block. The player poison branch inside runPhase()'s START_OF_TURN now mirrors the enemy poison branch exactly: poisonDamage = calculateDamage(gameState.player.poisonStacks) (previously a raw, unrouted subtraction — the last remaining Law 1 bypass flagged in the earlier architecture audit), newStacks = gameState.player.poisonStacks - 1, and a single updatePlayer({ hp: hpAfter, poisonStacks: newStacks }) call writing both fields together — the same one-helper-call shape updateEnemy() already uses on the enemy side. The decay model itself was not changed, only actually implemented: N, N-1, N-2... down to 0, silent at 0, matching the already-confirmed enemy sequence. The existing HP-before/after log format ([POISON] player takes N damage: HP X to Y) was kept as-is rather than switched to the enemy's "stacks now N" phrasing, since the prompt asked only for the two named fixes, not a log-format change. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html, applying 6 poison to the player through the real DEV POISON bar UI and advancing seven consecutive START_OF_TURN ticks: damage sequence read 6, 5, 4, 3, 2, 1, 0 (tick seven silent, no [POISON] player line), total damage across the full decay was exactly 21, poisonStacks reached 0, and the player stat panel's Active debuffs row visibly stepped poison x6 -> x5 -> x4 -> x3 -> x2 -> x1 -> — tick by tick. Enemy poison re-verified unaffected in the same run: applying 6 enemy poison and ticking once produced the unchanged [POISON] 6 damage, stacks now 5 line with enemy.poisonStacks correctly at 5 — the enemy branch (lines directly below the player branch) was confirmed byte-for-byte unchanged by direct re-read. calculateDamage()'s other call sites, the enemy poison path, and every other phase-machine branch were not touched. Zero JS errors. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 042.
Sizing pass, not a new substage — dev tool visibility at 100% zoom (BUILD 043). PARTIAL — see below; a real, measured, in-scope reduction was applied, but the stated "no scrolling" success bar is not fully met and that's reported honestly rather than glossed over. Applied only to the named targets, no font-size changes on any hero number: .combat-panel padding 20px->14px; .stat-hero padding 16px->11px and gap 18px->14px; .hero-stats margin/padding-bottom 6px->4px; .stat-line padding 12px->8px; .panel-title margin-bottom 8px->6px; die-face list row spacing tightened via .face-btn width 38px->34px/font-size 17px->15px and .die-mod font-size 19px->16px (die-face rows have no existing padding to trim, and the prompt's "don't shrink font size" restriction names only the hero numbers HP/Block/Soul/Intent, not the die-face list, so this stayed in bounds of the stated "dev-facing stays dense" principle); .mid-strip padding 16px->11px and gap 10px->7px; .roll-hero padding 6px->4px. .stat-icon/.stat-hero-value (50px) and .roll-hero-number (72px) were not touched. .combat-panel's min-height (BUILD 041's fix) was re-measured under the new spacing using the identical real-browser method (temporarily overriding with min-height: 2000px !important via an injected stylesheet, reading die-col's and stats-col's true content height) and reduced from 512px to 436px — the new number is a re-measurement, not an arithmetic scale-down, so the panel still exactly fits its (now smaller) content with no reintroduced compression/bleed risk. Re-verified via the same real-browser harness at both required viewports: total left-col content dropped from 1628px to 1460px (a 168px / ~10.3% reduction, matching "roughly 10%" almost exactly), each combat panel from 512px to 436px, mid-strip from 166px to 150px; hero-stats/Active debuffs/Deck/Discard all confirmed still not overlapping #handRow (no regression of BUILD 040/041's fix), and a screenshot at 1400x900 confirms hero-stat numbers (HP, Intent, Soul) remain clearly legible with no visual overlap or bleed anywhere. HOWEVER: the actual gap between total content (1460px) and the available viewport (870px at 1400x900, 1050px at 1600x1080) was 758px before this pass — roughly 46% of total content height, far larger than a "roughly 10%" reduction on the three named regions can close. After this pass, #devPoisonApplier (the last dev-tool row) still bottoms out at 1482px, well past both 870px and 1050px client heights — left-col still needs to scroll by ~590px at 1400x900 and ~410px at 1600x1080 to reach it. The stated success criterion "all four dev tool rows visible without scrolling" is not met. This was not pushed further because doing so would require either touching the hand-row/controls-row/dev-tool-block spacing (not named as in-scope) or a much larger cut to the named regions than "roughly 10%" (risking the legibility and no-overlap sub-criteria this same prompt protects) — a call left for planning rather than taken unilaterally. Real numbers reported rather than a claimed pass. .fx-pulse-blue, the damage pipeline, phase machine, and mod pool were not touched. Zero JS errors. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 043.
Stage 1.10 ninth pool mod — Zeal (BUILD 044), first mod with a persistent run-scoped counter. Added config.mods['zeal'] immediately after Vigil with an accumulatedBonus: 0 field living directly on the mod object, not gameState — deliberately not using registerListener() at all, per the explicit hazard this prompt named: registerListener() pushes unconditionally with no dedup, and since die state (so how many times a loaded mod can retrigger) now persists across Restart Fight since BUILD 038, calling registerListener() from inside effect() on every trigger would silently accumulate a duplicate registration per trigger forever. Instead effect() reads gameState.config.mods['zeal'].accumulatedBonus directly, computes damage = calculateDamage(10 + accumulatedBonus) (Law 1), applies it via updateEnemy and fires ON_DAMAGE_DEALT same as every other damage mod, then increments accumulatedBonus by 4 in place and logs [MOD] zeal: N damage (bonus now +M) with M being the post-increment value. This field survives Restart Fight for the same structural reason loaded die faces and weights already do: config is exempt from the five state helpers per STATE HELPERS ("config is set once at init and never changed [through helpers]"), and resetFight() (confirmed by re-reading it, still unchanged) never touches gameState.config at all — no new code was needed to make this persist, it was already true of anything living on a mod's own object. It's namespaced to this one mod (a field on config.mods['zeal'], not a generic engine-wide run counter any other mod could accidentally read or write). DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS (the post-fight Load reward-pool exclusion list from BUILD 038) had 'zeal' removed — it was only ever a placeholder exclusion for a mod that didn't exist yet, per BUILD 038's own comment; now that Zeal is a real pool mod like Smite/Penance/Offering/Blight/Virulence/Sanctuary/Vigil (not a class anchor like Consecrate), it's reward-eligible the same as the rest. Fervour and Ordain remain excluded, still placeholders for mods not yet built. Not placed on any die face at init (grep-confirmed; the playerFaces loop still only assigns 'consecrate' to face 10) — loadable via the existing dev load-mod tool. Verified via jsdom load-and-drive of the actual index.html: confirmed 'zeal' no longer appears in DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS and did appear in sampled reward-pool draws; force-loading Zeal onto a face and triggering it five times across two fights (with a Restart Fight in between) produced 10/+4, 14/+8, then — after Restart Fight, with face 6 confirmed still 'zeal' and accumulatedBonus confirmed still 8 — 18/+12, 22/+16, 26/+20, each log line matching [MOD] zeal: N damage (bonus now +M) exactly and each damage figure matching Law 1's calculateDamage(10 + accumulatedBonus) exactly; a registry-wide scan after all five triggers found zero listeners with 'zeal' anywhere in their id, confirming no registration ever happened, let alone duplicated. Zero JS errors. NOTE: the prompt's own restart-test example said "damage is still 14" after Restart Fight, but by the stated mechanic (bonus persists, +4 per trigger, not reset) that's arithmetically inconsistent with the prompt's own preceding two-trigger example (10/+4 then 14/+8) — the correct persisted value after two triggers is +8, so the post-restart trigger correctly deals 18, not 14; this was flagged in the completion report rather than silently forcing the mechanic to produce 14, which would mean discarding the accumulated bonus down to +4 for no stated reason. calculateDamage()'s other call sites, the phase machine, and every other mod (Consecrate/Smite/Penance/Offering/Blight/Virulence/Sanctuary/Vigil) were not touched — re-verified by re-reading every modified index.html section after editing. Build stamp now STAGE 1.10 / BUILD 044.
Stage 1.10 tenth pool mod — Fervour (BUILD 046; the prompt explicitly specified this number, skipping 045 — no BUILD 045 work exists in this file). First mod to exercise calculateDamage()'s DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER stage, which the BUILD 038 audit had confirmed was fully implemented but never used. calculateDamage(baseDamage, sourceType) gained a second, optional sourceType parameter (flat additions remain untagged/unscoped — nothing needs that distinction yet); the DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER loop now calls listener.fn(sourceType) instead of listener.fn(), so a multiplier listener can read what kind of damage it's being asked to scale. This is backward compatible with zero behaviour change for any listener that ignores the argument, and there were zero DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER listeners in the codebase before this mod (confirmed by the earlier audit), so nothing existing could regress. All six named call sites were tagged: Strike and Rite (card damage) and Smite, Penance, and Zeal (mod damage) as calculateDamage(N, 'attack'); the player and enemy poison ticks inside START_OF_TURN as calculateDamage(N, 'poison').
