Investigation — the run-record dieActionEvents gap: reproduced the reported shape (a Load offer shown, a mod picked, the run later abandoned) directly in real Chromium across three scenarios — (1) flushing while the card-reward panel was still open (the closest equivalent to a closed tab, since #startGameBtn is disabled whenever a die-action or card-reward panel is open, so a real misclick can't reach New Run mid-offer), (2) the normal flow (dismiss the reward, return to the map, click New Run), and (3) an elite's two-die-action loop (Load+pick for the first action, Skip for the second, then abandon). All three preserved dieActionEvents correctly through to the flushed line in every case. Could not reproduce the gap under the current code; no fix applied, since applying one without a confirmed defect would be guessing, which the prompt explicitly ruled out. Build stamp: corrected from STAGE 2.41/BUILD 109 (unchanged since BUILD 109, eight builds ago, despite BUILD 116 also touching index.html) to STAGE 2.43/BUILD 117. CLAUDE.md's WHO EDITS THIS FILE section gained a standing instruction: every build updates the stamp to its own stage/build number, in the same build, regardless of what else that build touches — the one standing exception to "don't touch files the build doesn't need to." tests/facts.test.js: three new tests (34/34, up from 31/31) — a two-mod row renders both names and both counts (and asserts no `.die-mod-stack` element exists, guarding against a regression back to the vertical layout), every mod in the pool has a MOD_DESCRIPTION entry (13/13), and the die column is still twenty rows, all the same height, with a two-mod face present. tests/mods.test.js: unchanged, 20/20 before and after (no mod behaviour touched). VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, zero console/page errors: weight 2 (face 2) and weight 4 (face 3) shown side by side against a weight-1 control (face 4) — real screenshot pixel comparison confirmed all three genuinely differ, and a 2x-scale screenshot (tests/build117_final_weights_row.png, individual crops tests/build117_final_weight2.png/weight4.png) shows the difference plainly: weight 2 reads as a moderate blue fill under the digit, weight 4 reads as a much stronger one. A two-mod face (Consecrate+Virulence, both with double-digit trigger counts — the worst case) measured: die column still exactly 20 rows, every row including the two-mod one at an identical 19px height (tests/build117_final_twomod_row.png), rendering as "Con… #15 Virul… #27" on one line with correct spacing after the .die-mod-second fix. Every one of the twelve reward-eligible mods (Consecrate, the anchor, is checked separately since it's excluded from Load offers) was forced into a real Load offer and its hover-tip text read back non-empty and matching its own MOD_DESCRIPTION entry.

Stage 2.44 — KI-19, the run record's per-mod trigger counts, plus Load All (BUILD 118) — landed. CAUSE, found not assumed: collectTriggerCountsByMod() (run-and-map.js:297-308 before this build) wrote `counts[f.modId] = faceCount` and `counts[f.modId2] = faceCount` — plain assignment, not accumulation — while looping every face on the die. When a mod id sits on only one face (the only shape the real Load flow can ever produce — DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS plus the "already on the die" check in dieActionChooseLoad() refuse a mod that's already loaded anywhere), assignment and summation are indistinguishable and the bug is invisible. When the same mod sits on more than one face — which devLoadMod() (dev-tools.js), used for manual testing, has no guard against — each face processed just overwrote the previous one's count, so only the LAST face visited (ascending face order) survived; every earlier face's count for that mod was silently discarded. This exactly matches both reported symptoms: consecrate:1 (against faces reading #1/#2/#4) is the last-processed Consecrate face's own count of 1, the #2 and #4 faces' counts thrown away; blight:0 (against faces reading up to #4) is the last-processed Blight face happening to read 0. Grepped for the same collect-and-sum shape elsewhere in the run record: fightRoundsCol/totalRounds (buildRunRecordLine()) map/reduce over an append-only array (recordFightRoundEnd() pushes, never keys-and-overwrites) and eventsCol likewise maps an append-only array — neither has anywhere a value could collide and be overwritten. collectTriggerCountsByMod() was the only place with this fault.

FIX: counts[modId] now accumulates (`counts[modId] = (counts[modId]||0) + faceCount`) for both a face's modId and modId2, and every real mod (Object.keys(gameState.config.mods)) is seeded at 0 before the face loop runs, so a mod on zero faces this run reports 0 in the trigger-counts column instead of being absent from it. RUN RECORD (CLAUDE.md) updated — the old text's own claim that "there is nothing to merge or collide" was the same wrong assumption the bug lived under; corrected to state plainly that the real Load flow's own duplicate-prevention is what made the fault invisible, not that duplicates can't happen.

LOAD ALL (#devLoadAllBtn, beside the existing #devLoadModBtn in the dev-load row): devLoadAll() (dev-tools.js) reads the mod currently selected in #devModSelect (the same dropdown devLoadMod() already reads) and walks faces 2-19 only — never face 1 or face 20. Blank face -> fills modId (first slot). Already-one-mod face -> fills modId2 (second slot). Already-two-mod face -> skipped, no write. A dev action: no updateRunRecord() call anywhere in the function, so pressing it never adds anything to gameState.runRecord.dieActionEvents the way a real Load does. Logs "[DEV] dev-load all: <mod name> filled onto N face(s), M face(s) skipped (already holding two mods)" — the word is always "dev-load," never bare "load," per the prompt's own instruction (the pre-existing "[CLICK] Load All" button-name log line, which every button already produces for its own label, is a different, unrelated log line and was left alone). If nothing is available to fill (every eligible face already holds two mods), it writes nothing and logs "[DEV] dev-load all: nothing available to fill (N face(s) already holding two mods)".

tests/facts.test.js: three new tests (37/37, up from 34/34) — a mod on more than one face sums across those faces (blight on three faces with hand-set counts 1/2/4, must read 7), a mod in a second slot is counted toward the same sum as the first slot (consecrate as modId on face 10 plus modId2 on another face, 3+5 must read 8), and a mod on no face records zero rather than being omitted (a fresh die's elevation entry must exist and read 0, and the object must carry all thirteen keys). tests/mods.test.js: unchanged, 20/20 before and after (no mod behaviour touched).

VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, zero console/page errors: built a die with 'offering' (a non-damage mod, so driving several real rounds can't accidentally kill the opening enemy and derail the phase-machine loop — a real snag hit while writing this script with 'blight' instead, whose cumulative poison killed the enemy mid-test) on three faces (2, 5, 12), then forced real rolls through the actual dispatch path: face 2 twice, face 5 once, face 12 three times. On-screen badges read #2 / #1 / #3 (screenshot tests/build118_trigger_sum_die.png) — sum 6. collectTriggerCountsByMod().offering read 6. Recorded count equals the on-screen sum: 6 = 6. Load All: selected Sanctuary, pressed once — 18 faces filled (every face 2-19), 0 skipped, face 1 and face 20 completely unchanged (byte-identical before/after), gameState.runRecord.dieActionEvents unchanged (still empty — Load All never counts toward the run), log line read exactly "[DEV] dev-load all: Sanctuary filled onto 18 face(s), 0 face(s) skipped (already holding two mods)". Face 10 (the anchor) correctly kept consecrate in modId and got sanctuary in modId2, not overwritten. Pressed again: correctly filled every face's still-open second slot (17 faces — one press only fills each face's first available slot, so the die isn't fully saturated until two presses; this is correct, expected behaviour, not a bug caught while writing the verification script). Pressed a third time, with the die now genuinely full on every eligible face: wrote nothing at all (byte-identical faces before/after) and logged "[DEV] dev-load all: nothing available to fill (18 face(s) already holding two mods)".

Stage 2.45 (BUILD 119) — a measurement session, not a feature build: re-measured the bot win rate D-08 recorded at 34% at BUILD 105, eight builds later. No design or policy change to the bot — resolveDieAction() and LOAD_PRIORITY in tests/autoplay.js are byte-for-byte the same this build ends as they were when it started; the only edits are run-record plumbing.

PRE-EDIT GREP (per the prompt's explicit gate, posted and approved before any edit): three questions, two stop conditions triggered. (1) resolveDieAction()'s face picker only ever targets `f.modId === null` faces when Loading — it structurally can never pick an already-loaded face's second slot, and does correctly fall back to Strengthen face 20 whenever no blank face remains (confirmed: this never actually happens within one run — see below). (2) autoplay.js had zero references to updateRunRecord/runRecord/flushRunRecord/collectTriggerCountsByMod anywhere — it wrote only its own separate tests/autoplay_results.csv, never touching the BUILD 109 run record at all, so its runs produced no run-record line, bot-tagged or otherwise. (3) zero references to any state removed/renamed by BUILDs 106-118 (triggerCounts, .weight, strengthenFace) — every gameState.*/GAME_CONFIG.*/function reference cross-checked clean against current js/. Approved fix: add run-record plumbing only, so the bot's runs land in the same record a human's do (RUN RECORD, CLAUDE.md, already documented `source` as a hook for exactly this "future headless writer" — the gap this build closes, not a new mechanic).

THE EDIT (tests/autoplay.js, two changes, both plumbing, nothing else): (1) `page.evaluate(() => updateRunRecord({ source: 'bot' }))` inserted in playRun() immediately after the `#startGameBtn` click — startNewRun()'s own synchronous click handler has already called resetRunRecord() (source: 'human' by default) by the time page.click() resolves, so this always lands after that reset and correctly overwrites it. (2) `browser.newPage()` (freshPage(), used once per run) was discovered THIS BUILD to open each run in its own new, fully isolated browser context — a real finding, not assumed: a second page opened via browser.newPage() cannot see localStorage written by an earlier page's run, confirmed empirically when a first attempt at extracting run-record lines after a batch read back zero lines. So a human's real persistent-profile accumulation across runs doesn't happen for this automated batch; playRun() was extended to read its own run's localStorage (`dieRunRecordLines`) before its page closes and return it as `row.runRecordLines`, so a caller can accumulate every run's line itself. Both changes are pure extraction/tagging — no line in resolveDieAction(), LOAD_PRIORITY, or any other decision code was touched. `module.exports` (playRun/runBatch/etc., guarded behind `if (require.main === module)` around the existing CLI body) was also added so a separate measurement script could reuse these exact, unmodified functions rather than re-implementing the policy — `node tests/autoplay.js` on its own is unaffected, confirmed by an ad hoc CLI run producing the byte-identical CSV line before and after this change.

tests/mods.test.js: 20/20 before, 20/20 after. tests/facts.test.js: 37/37 before, 37/37 after. Neither test file was touched; both re-run after the edit per the prompt's discipline.

MEASUREMENT (asBuilt, riteHeal=20 — BUILD 105's "as built" arm), 400 seeded games, seeds 1-400 (BUILD 105's own reproducibility protocol used seeds 1-100 per combination — this batch's 1-400 is a wider, not identical, seed range; seeds 1-100 are a subset in common). Every number below is stated both from autoplay.js's own row-derived computation and independently recomputed by parsing the 400 flushed, bot-tagged run-record lines themselves — the two agreed exactly everywhere they overlap (win count: 119 both ways).

Win rate: 119/400 = 29.75% (tool-summary and run-record-recomputed agree exactly), against D-08's 34% baseline at BUILD 105 — a real, if moderate, drop over the eight intervening builds. Kill-round distribution for wins (boss fight's own round count, not the run's summed total — an error caught and corrected mid-session, see below): round 1:6, 2:4, 3:12, 4:16, 5:24, 6:25, 7:15, 8:10, 9:4, 10:1, 11:2 (sums to 119). Median loaded faces at the boss (from run-record dieActionEvents Load picks, runs that reached the boss only, n=226): 6. Second-slot-usage count: 0 — not because the feature went unused by a human, but because resolveDieAction()'s face picker (untouched, unmodified this build) only ever selects `f.modId === null` faces, so it structurally can never target an already-loaded face's second slot. Count of runs Strengthening face 20 at least once: 0 — dieActionEvents never records a "strengthen" entry at all (dieActionChooseStrengthen()/dieActionPickStrengthenFace() write nothing to gameState.runRecord.dieActionEvents, grep-confirmed against rendering.js), so this can't be read off a run-record line directly; derived instead from the batch's own observed data — the most Load events any single run recorded was 7, against 18 always-available blank die faces (2-19), so resolveDieAction()'s hasBlank check (untouched) never went false within any of the 400 runs and the Strengthen else-branch never fired — a design/schema report, not run-record blindness. Total trigger count per mod, summed across all 400 runs: consecrate 1414, smite 547, penance 131, offering 277, blight 547, virulence 202, sanctuary 472, vigil 47, zeal 334, fervour 151, ordain 496, anthem 6, elevation 11.

CORRECTION CAUGHT MID-SESSION: the first pass's kill-round distribution used totalRounds (the run's fightRounds column summed across every fight — opening plus every lane fight plus the boss) instead of the boss fight's own round count, producing nonsense values (28-50) for a "which round did the kill land on" question. Caught by inspection before reporting, fixed by re-parsing the fightRounds column's own last "Boss:N" entry from the same already-captured 401 real run-record lines — no re-run of the 400 games was needed or done.

VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, zero console/page/assertion errors on the substantive checks: one seeded verification run (seed 42, asBuilt, riteHeal 20) produced exactly one run-record line, source=bot, non-empty triggerCounts. The 400-run batch produced exactly 400 bot-tagged run-record lines (400/400, no drops, no duplicates) alongside its unchanged tests/autoplay_results.csv output. Copy-button check: in one real page/context, a human-tagged run and a bot-tagged run (via the same source-tagging hook used in playRun(), both abandoned immediately through the real flushRunRecord('abandoned') path) were both flushed, `#copyRunRecordBtn` clicked, and the OS clipboard read back containing both a `human,` line and a `bot,` line together — confirming the two sources genuinely land in the same copy, not just that bot lines are technically readable. D-40: every stored line from this session (1 verification run + 400 batch runs + 2 copy-button demo lines = 403) written to a new CSV, run_record_master.csv, in the project root (C:/Users/figja/Die_v1) — 404 lines total including its header, confirmed by `wc -l`. A first attempt at this step's `#startGameBtn` click hung on a `confirm()` dialog (run.outcome still 'active' from the first demo run, un-handled by the script) and was fixed by registering a `page.on('dialog', d => d.accept())` handler before the retry — a script bug in the verification harness, not a game bug.

CLAUDE.md, HISTORY.md, index.html (this build's own documentation/build-stamp update, backed up the same timestamp: backups/CLAUDE.md.bak_20260911_222705, backups/HISTORY.md.bak_20260911_222705, backups/index.html.bak_20260911_222705). Stage 2.46 (BUILD 112) — "make verification honest": closes KI-14, KI-9, KI-2, and builds the registry inspector from REG-E2. A measurement-and-fix session, not a numbers/gameplay build.

KI-14 (tests/facts.test.js circularity): the Notion Register's own KI-14 entry states plainly that facts.test.js "asserts the FACTS comment against GAME_CONFIG, and both were written from this page; a wrong fact passes." GREP-AND-REPORT (before any edit): classified all 37 existing tests by whether they exercise real game action/computation versus purely echoing a documented number against GAME_CONFIG. The prompt's assumed ~23 circular/14 behavioural split did not match either a strict count (9 circular: F01/F02/F04/F16/F17-18/F20/F21/F24/F25) or a generous one (14 circular, counting any test with at least one redundant cfg-vs-hardcoded-literal assertion even where real behaviour is also checked) — reported as a material discrepancy per the prompt's own stop condition. User confirmed: the ~23/14 estimate came from KI-14's own wording on the Notion page, not from an actual count of the file — the estimate was wrong, the file's real split is 14 circular / 25 behaviourally-anchored (of 37, pre-BUILD-112). FIX (per explicit follow-up instruction: label, don't weaken): a new `specOnlyEqual()`/`specOnlyDeepEqual()` pair (facts.test.js) performs the identical assert.strictEqual/deepStrictEqual check but tags it into a `specOnlyChecks` array and prints `[SPEC-ONLY] <test> — <label>` at the moment it runs; every genuinely redundant "documented number vs GAME_CONFIG" assertion across those 14 tests (18 assertion-sites total: F01, F02, F04, F16 ×2, F17/F18, F24, F25, F27 ×2, F03, F05, F06/F07/F08 ×3, F10, F19, F23) now routes through it — no assertion that exercises real behaviour (a forced roll, a real card effect(), a real phase transition, a real DOM/render check) was touched or weakened. The final summary line now reports the SPEC-ONLY count and the tests it came from separately from the plain pass count, so "37/37 passed" (now 39/39, see KI-9/KI-2 below) can no longer be read as "every fact independently behaviourally verified."

Step 4 (tests/autoplay.js mod selection — grep and report only, bot policy untouched): `LOAD_PRIORITY` (autoplay.js:165) is a hardcoded 9-mod list (blight, smite, sanctuary, zeal, ordain, offering, fervour, penance, vigil). Neither anthem nor elevation appears anywhere in the file, in `LOAD_PRIORITY` or any other list — the priority list predates BUILD 113 (Anthem) and BUILD 114 (Elevation); both mods can only ever be chosen via the `dieActionMods[0]` fallback (none of the 3 offered mods matched the priority list), matching BUILD 119's own 6 Anthem / 11 Elevation vs 547 Smite trigger counts. Also newly found by this same grep: `virulence` is missing from the list too (12 Load-eligible mods minus 9 listed = 3 missing, not 2) — reported alongside anthem/elevation. Not edited, per the prompt's explicit instruction — changing the bot's policy would invalidate the BUILD 105/119 win-rate baseline comparison.

Notion Register read (Reconciliation — checkable against code view, "In force" rows with Checkable=Grep|Playwright): confirms KI-14's own text and supplied D-08's real BUILD 105 numbers (34%/38% as-built, 5%/6% elites-and-rites-only, the bot being a weaker reference player than Fergus at 4/9 human wins) — read for context; the view itself carries no circular/behavioural split number for facts.test.js, consistent with the ~23/14 estimate being KI-14's own prose, not a count.

KI-9 (resetFight() lost its clearListeners('fight') call in BUILD 092): checked, not assumed. clearFightScopedState()/resetFight() (run-and-map.js) genuinely call neither clearListeners('fight') (correctly — dead since BUILD 092, no listener has ever used clearOn:'fight') NOR clearListeners('turn'). A probe script confirmed empirically: a real Fervour trigger (registers a genuine clearOn:'turn' DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER listener) is STILL present in gameState.registry.listeners the instant a fight ends mid-turn (forced win via runPhase()'s own top-of-function guard) — KI-9's premise is real, not stale. But it is gone by the time the next real fight has actually started, because every real fight-transition path (beginFightFromSlot() -> startFreshTurnPaused() -> runPhase('START_OF_TURN')) synchronously runs a fresh clearListeners('turn') (phase-machine.js:204) before any player action is possible — the gap is real but never reaches real gameplay, since nothing dispatches DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER from the intervening post-win die-action/card-reward screens. New test, facts.test.js: "KI-9: a stale turn-scoped listener does not survive into a second real fight" — asserts BOTH halves honestly (present right after the win; gone once fight 2 has started), through real forcePlayerRoll()/updateEnemy({hp:0})/nextPhase()/chooseLane()/enterSlot() calls, not a synthetic listener.

KI-2 (playCard() run.status guard): one line added at the top of playCard() (cards-mods.js) — `if (gameState.run.status !== 'active') { log('[CARD] cannot play — run is not active'); return; }` — mirroring the same guard callListeners() (listener-registry.js) already applies to every hook dispatch, extended here to playCard(), the one direct state-mutating entry point that doesn't go through a listener. New test, facts.test.js: "KI-2: playCard() rejects a call attempted after the run has halted" — forces a win, attempts playCard(0), asserts it doesn't throw, logs the rejection, and leaves soul/hand completely unchanged.

REG-E2 (registry inspector): new dev-drawer panel, #registryInspector/#registryInspectorContent (index.html, same hidden-block/.expanded-toggle pattern as the existing State Inspector), wired via #registryInspectorToggleBtn (bootstrap.js) and rendered by a new renderRegistryInspector() (rendering.js), called from refreshInspector() alongside every other panel refresh. Read-only, straight off gameState.registry.listeners (KI-3's own premise — the registry already lives on gameState, listener-registry.js's registerListener()/clearListeners() read and write it directly, nothing new to expose) — grouped by hook, then within each hook by clearOn ('permanent' then 'turn'; 'fight' deliberately gets no group since it is dead). DEV MODE (CLAUDE.md) already described this exact tool ("Registry inspector: all listeners grouped by hook and clearOn") before this build — a standing spec/code gap this build closes, no text change needed there.

tests/facts.test.js: two new tests (39/39, up from 37/37: KI-9 leak test, KI-2 guard test) plus the 18 SPEC-ONLY relabelings inside 14 existing tests (no count change from those, since nothing was added or removed, only labelled). tests/mods.test.js: unchanged, 20/20 before and after.

VERIFIED, real Chromium via Playwright, zero console/page errors, zero assertion failures: (1) registry inspector opened via the real dev-drawer toggle chain, mid-fight-1 (after a real Fervour trigger) shows `DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER (1) ... turn (1) fervour_double_attacks` in its live text content; after forcing fight 1's win, skipping the real post-win screens, and entering fight 2 via real chooseLane()/enterSlot(), the same panel shows `DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER (0) ... turn (0) (none)` — the stale entry is gone, confirming no stale fight-scoped listener survives into a second real fight, in the actual rendered UI, not just gameState. (2) KI-2 guard: forced the run to a halt (fight 2's own forced win), called `playCard(0)` directly in the real page — did not throw, logged exactly "[CARD] cannot play — run is not active" as the log panel's last line, left soul (3) and hand length (5) completely unchanged. Screenshots: tests/build112_registry_inspector_midfight.png, tests/build112_registry_inspector_after_transition.png, tests/build112_ki2_guard_rejection.png (full-page captures; the dev drawer sits below the fold at this viewport height, so the definitive verification is the DOM textContent reads and assertions above, not the screenshot framing). Zero console errors, zero page errors across the whole script.

Grepped `stacks` (case-insensitive) across every file in /js/, index.html, and tests/. Findings, sorted by the prompt's three named categories:
- **Log strings**: two bare occurrences, both in cards-mods.js (`enemy_buff_dispatch`'s player-poison line, and the boss's `ENEMY_NAT_ONE` self-poison line) and one in phase-machine.js (`START_OF_TURN`'s enemy poison-tick line) — all three read "...stacks now N" with no status word attached to that clause (the sentence's earlier "poison" reference doesn't save it — a second stacking status added later would make the same clause ambiguous, which is exactly what D-53 exists to prevent). Every other log line already named poison beside every "stacks"/count it printed (e.g. reckoning's "N poison stacks", blight/virulence's "applied N poison" with no bare "stacks" at all) and was left untouched.
- **MOD_DESCRIPTION**: audited all thirteen entries (rendering.js) — none contain the word "stacks" (blight/virulence read "applied 6 poison" / "applied 3 poison, doubled"). Nothing to fix.
- **GAME_CONFIG comments**: F19 ("enemy poison buff 5 stacks") and F23 ("self-poison flat 5 stacks") in config.js's header, mirrored verbatim from 1 State's FACTS block — both reworded to "N stacks of poison" and pushed to both copies in this build (no value change, wording only).
Variable names (poisonStacks, ENEMY_BUFF_POISON_STACKS, ENEMY_NAT_ONE_SELF_POISON) and UI/HTML text (both already compliant — "Poison Stacks" stat label, "apply poison stacks" dev-tools comment) were left alone, per the prompt's explicit "variable names are untouched" and the fact they already name the status.

LOAD_PRIORITY (tests/autoplay.js): queried the live Notion mod-pool database directly (collection://d28e3e13-e92e-4971-bf4f-da0dd642afe5, sorted by Rank) — Fervour 1, Sanctuary 2, Virulence 3, Smite 4, Blight 5, Elevation 6, Zeal 7, Offering 8, Anthem 9, Ordain 10, Penance 11, Vigil 12; Consecrate carries no rank (the anchor, never offered). Replaced the old 9-mod LOAD_PRIORITY (missing Virulence, Anthem, Elevation; special-cased Virulence-after-Blight) with this flat 12-mod array in rank order, and simplified resolveDieAction()'s bot pick logic in tests/autoplay.js to a plain walk of the list — no heuristic left, per the checkpoint's own "nothing left to decide" framing.

NEW TEST: "BUILD 123: LOAD_PRIORITY ranks every loadable mod exactly once" (tests/facts.test.js) — opens a live page, reads gameState.config.mods, asserts LOAD_PRIORITY contains no duplicates and its sorted contents exactly equal the loadable set (every real mod except consecrate). This reuses the same assertion tests/autoplay.js's own CLI already ran once per invocation (assertEveryLoadableModIsRanked, exported) — added to facts.test.js too so a mod added later without updating LOAD_PRIORITY fails the standing regression suite immediately, not only a manual `node tests/autoplay.js` run.

CAUGHT BUG in the measurement harness itself, not the game: the first baseline attempt was invoked as `node tests/autoplay.js --seed 1 --dieRewardRule asBuilt --riteHeal 20 --runs 400` (space-separated flags) and silently ran the file's *default* full verification protocol instead (a reproducibility check plus a 100-run×4-combination sweep) — traced to parseArgs() (tests/autoplay.js) only matching `--flag=value` via `/^--([a-zA-Z]+)=(.+)$/`, so every space-separated arg was silently dropped and `args` stayed empty, falling through to the `else` branch. Re-invoked correctly as `node tests/autoplay.js --seed=1 --dieRewardRule=asBuilt --riteHeal=20 --runs=400` (seeds 1-400, single combination) — this produced the real baseline below. Also newly found while diagnosing this: tests/autoplay.js's own `const BUILD = 105;` (line 158, used only to stamp the CSV's `build` column) has never been bumped since the file was written at BUILD 105 — the same class of stale-build-stamp bug KI-18 closed for the on-screen stamp, now found in this file's own CSV output column. Not fixed this build (out of scope — the CSV column is informational only, doesn't affect the win-rate arithmetic, and no KI number was requested for it); flagged here for a future build to pick up. FIXED AT BUILD 124: see CLAUDE.md's BUILD 124 CONFIRMED WORKING entry.

BASELINE: `node tests/autoplay.js --seed=1 --dieRewardRule=asBuilt --riteHeal=20 --runs=400`, 400 real Playwright-driven runs, seeds 1-400, asBuilt, riteHeal 20 — the exact methodology D-08's 34% (BUILD 105) and BUILD 119's 29.75% remeasurement both used. Result: **win rate 25.8% (103 won / 297 lost, confirmed by counting tests/autoplay_results.csv's own outcome column for the run's 400 appended rows)**, median arrival HP 38, median faces at boss 7, median final round 6. This replaces the 34% figure D-08 currently names; win rate has now fallen at every one of the three measurements (105: 34%, 119: 29.75%, 123: 25.8%) even as the bot's own mod-selection policy got strictly more complete (9 mods → all 12 ranked) — worth a design conversation, not something this build's scope covers.

PROOF, two independent lines of evidence, cross-checked: (1) backups/ holds four historical snapshots of this exact file — backups/tests_20260911_151259/ and backups/audit_20260911_161708/ both show exactly 402 data rows (Sept 11, before that day's session and again mid-session, unchanged); backups/tests_20260918_175152/, backups/tests_20260918_180037/, and backups/tests_20260918_180545/ (three separate Sept 18 pre-build backups, before BUILD 120/121/123 respectively) all show exactly 804 data rows, unchanged across all three — so rows 403-804 were added in one sitting between Sept 11 16:17 and Sept 18 17:51, and rows 805+ only after the last of those three backups (18:05:45, immediately pre-BUILD-123). (2) Row content at every claimed boundary matches each build's own documented batch composition exactly, not just the row count: rows 1-2 and 401-402 match BUILD 105's "2 reproducibility runs (seed 42 ×2) + 400-run 4-combination sweep (asBuilt/elitesAndRitesOnly × riteHeal 20/25, seeds 1-100 each)" write-up (Stage 2.36, HISTORY.md) byte-for-byte, including the elitesAndRitesOnly/riteHeal-25/seed-100 pair at row 402; rows 403-404 and 803-804 match BUILD 119's "2 reproducibility runs + single-combination 400-run baseline, seeds 1-400, asBuilt/riteHeal 20" write-up (Stage 2.45) exactly, ending at seed 400; rows 805-1206 match BUILD 123's own CAUGHT BUG description of its accidental default-protocol run (reproducibility pair + the same 4-combination/100-seed sweep shape as BUILD 105's, confirmed by row 1000 reading riteHeal 25 and row 1100/1200 reading elitesAndRitesOnly) and rows 1207-1606 match its corrected re-invocation (`--seed=1 --dieRewardRule=asBuilt --riteHeal=20 --runs=400`, straight seeds 1-400, no reproducibility pair) exactly, landing on seed 400 at the file's last row. Every one of the three boundary edges was read directly off the file's own columns, not inferred from row count alone.
Where this proof was NOT available, nothing was touched: no row range in this file falls outside the three ranges above (100% of the 1,606 existing rows were provably attributable), so nothing is reported as unreliable this build — but the method above is the bar any future ambiguous range must clear before being relabelled; a range that can't be matched this precisely stays as-is and gets reported unreliable rather than guessed.

D-40 DISCREPANCY, found and reported, not silently resolved: this session's setup named "the run-record CSV (D-40)" as the file with the mislabeled build column. MECHANIC: buildAct(actNumber) (run-and-map.js) now takes the 1-based act number and bakes that act's own scaled numbers into every enemy at build time — hp and intentMin/intentMax via Math.ceil(base × GAME_CONFIG.ACT_HP_MULTIPLIER/ACT_INTENT_MULTIPLIER[actNumber-1]), and the enemy buff's own poison amount (new gameState.enemy.buffPoisonStacks field, copied from the slot by beginFightFromSlot() exactly like hp/intentMin/intentMax) via the same intent multiplier, also Math.ceil'd. Multipliers are [1.0, 1.4, 1.9] (HP) and [1.0, 1.2, 1.45] (intent) across acts 1/2/3 — act 1's are both 1.0, so buildAct(1)'s output is byte-identical to the pre-125 buildAct()'s. The enemy Nat 1 self-poison (GAME_CONFIG.ENEMY_NAT_ONE_SELF_POISON) is untouched — flat 5, unscaled, per the prompt's explicit instruction. enemy_buff_dispatch (cards-mods.js) now reads gameState.enemy.buffPoisonStacks instead of the flat GAME_CONFIG.ENEMY_BUFF_POISON_STACKS constant directly. Die face layouts (F20/F21) are unchanged per act.

TRANSITION: runPhase()'s win branch (phase-machine.js) — a boss win only ends the whole run in VICTORY (D-22, no reward) when gameState.run.actNumber >= GAME_CONFIG.ACTS (3). Every earlier act's boss win instead falls into the same reward flow (dieActionsRemaining = GAME_CONFIG.DIE_REWARDS.SINGLE — 'Boss' is never 'Elite', so never the two-action grant — then openDieActionScreen(), followed by the usual card reward) any other fight win gets; boss_defeated still plays either way. advanceRun()'s new 'boss' branch (run-and-map.js, reached only once that reward flow closes, i.e. never for the final act) increments actNumber, rebuilds gameState.run.act via buildAct(actNumber), and resets currentSlot to 'opening' and lane to null — the same three fields startNewRun() itself sets for act 1. Player hp, die and ownedCards are untouched (no heal between acts, D-27).

UI: gameState.run.actNumber shown on the map screen title ("ACT N MAP", renderMapScreen()) and a new #actStamp element on the fight screen (set by refreshInspector()), both reusing .panel-title, no new palette.

NEW TESTS, tests/facts.test.js: "F31: enemy HP/intent and the poison-buff amount scale per act with Math.ceil, act 1 unchanged" (reads buildAct(1)/buildAct(2)/buildAct(3) directly — a real page-global function, not a second hand-typed copy — and checks every documented number: act 2 opening HP 70, act 3 boss HP 190, act 3's 85-HP normal at 162, act 2/3 poison-buff amounts 6/8, and every act 1 number unchanged); "F32: acts 1-2 boss wins grant a reward and start the next act; only act 3 is VICTORY" (forces each boss win the same real-code-path way every other win test in this file does — updateEnemy({hp:0}); nextPhase() — then skips through the reward panels exactly as a player Skip-clicking would, asserting actNumber/currentSlot/lane/outcome after each act, and that the act 3 boss win alone sets outcome:'won' with no die/card reward panel at all).

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 125's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the three-act mechanic/transition write-up, in full, including its own FOUND-NOT-FIXED build-stamp note) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

MECHANIC: dieActionChooseLoad() (rendering.js) already computed `eligible` — every mod in gameState.config.mods except the anchor (consecrate) and anything currently loaded on the die (either slot) — before drawing its 3-mod offer. Once eligible.length < 3, a real 3-mod offer can't be built: the old code just returned whatever shuffle().slice(0,3) gave it, silently showing a short offer (never a duplicated one — slice on a too-small array doesn't repeat elements — but "short" is exactly the failure D-54 exists to close). Fixed by checking eligible.length first: below 3, it logs the conversion and calls dieActionChooseStrengthen() directly, returning before dieActionMods or dieActionStep are ever touched — Load can no longer present any offer, short or otherwise, once the pool runs this low. No UI change: the Load/Strengthen/Skip buttons at the 'choose' step are unchanged; this is a click-time redirect inside dieActionChooseLoad() itself, not a disabled or relabelled button — a UI-level indicator was out of this build's scope.

ALSO FIXED (carried over from BUILD 125, found there, not fixed there): index.html's #buildStamp stage text was stuck at "STAGE 2.51" through BUILD 125 despite that build changing GAME_CONFIG.BUILD to 125 and BUILD 117's standing instruction to bump it every build — the on-screen BUILD *number* was still correct throughout (GAME_CONFIG.BUILD-driven, per KI-18's own fix), only the hand-typed stage text lagged. Now "STAGE 2.53", covering both the missed 2.52 and this build's own bump.

NEW TEST, tests/facts.test.js — "D-54: with fewer than three unloaded mods left, a die reward offers Strengthen, not Load": loads ten of the twelve loadable mods directly onto ten distinct die faces (updateDie(), Law A3-compliant, not the real Load flow — which would itself now be blocked by this same fix well before the pool got this low), confirms exactly two (penance, vigil) remain unloaded, then calls openDieActionScreen(); dieActionChooseLoad() and asserts dieActionStep reads 'strengthen_pick_face' (not 'load_pick_mod') with dieActionMods left empty — a behavioural assertion against the real conversion path, not a comment/config comparison. Superseded by BUILD 127's own rewritten version of this test (button-visibility assertion plus a defence-in-depth direct-call check).

VERIFIED: tests/mods.test.js 20/20 before and after (unchanged — no mod behaviour touched). tests/facts.test.js 44/44 before this build's edit, 45/45 after (one new test).

Stage 2.54 (BUILD 127) — the bot plays all three acts, checkpoint 2 closes. Third of the 125-127 batch, batched with stop-on-failure. Mid-build correction on the user's own explicit instruction: stopped the 400-run baseline, revised BUILD 126's D-54 fix from a click-time redirect to hiding the Load button outright, updated the bot and its test to match, ran a 20-run smoke check instead of the full baseline, then closed docs.

THREE-ACT BOT: tests/autoplay.js's playRun() no longer stops at one boss — a new playOneAct(page, dieRewardRule, riteHeal) helper plays exactly one act (its own opening fight through its own boss, always the elite/upper lane, same policy as before) and playRun() loops it from act 1 through GAME_CONFIG.ACTS (read live off the page, not hardcoded), breaking on any loss and stopping in true victory once the final act's boss falls. A non-final act's boss win still resolves its reward flow inside playOneAct() itself before the loop continues — advanceRun()'s own act-transition (BUILD 125) has already reset gameState.run.currentSlot to 'opening' by the time the next loop iteration calls enterSlot('opening', null) again. tests/autoplay_results.csv gained an actReached column (appended, not inserted — every existing column keeps its position): the highest act number a run actually played a fight in. All 1,616 rows written before this build were blanked in that column (never guessed — see the migration below); facesAtBoss/faceListAtBoss/arrivalHp/bossHpAtEnd keep meaning "this run's own last boss fought," now generalised to whichever act that was, exactly as they always meant it about the one boss there used to be.

CRASH FOUND MID-BUILD, NOT IN THE ORIGINAL SCOPE: the very first real multi-act baseline attempt (seed 1, run 16 of an attempted 400) crashed inside renderDieActionPanel() — `gameState.config.mods[dieActionChosenModId].name` on an undefined mod — because tests/autoplay.js's own resolveDieAction() always called dieActionChooseLoad() whenever a blank face existed, with no idea that BUILD 126's D-54 fix could silently convert that call into a Strengthen once the pool ran low (dieActionMods left empty), then tried to pick dieActionMods[0] === undefined as a mod to load. This scenario had never been reachable in a single-act run's Load-offer volume; playing three full acts made it real. Reported to the user along with the fact that BUILD 126's own fix, while behaviourally correct, was a silent click-time redirect a real player would never see coming — the user then directed the actual fix.

D-54 REVISED (Load hidden, not redirected): renderDieActionPanel()'s 'choose' step now reads a new shared eligibleLoadModIds() (rendering.js, factored out of dieActionChooseLoad()'s own eligibility filter — the same list, one source) and simply does not render the Load button at all once it's below 3 — Strengthen and Skip are unaffected. dieActionChooseLoad() keeps its convert-to-Strengthen behaviour as a defensive fallback for a direct call (dev tooling, a future bug), not the primary mechanism any more. tests/autoplay.js's resolveDieAction() now checks the identical eligibleLoadModIds().length >= 3 condition before ever calling dieActionChooseLoad() — mirroring exactly what a human would see (no Load button to click) — closing the crash at its root rather than patching around the symptom.

TEST REWRITTEN, tests/facts.test.js — "D-54: with fewer than three unloaded mods left, the die action panel shows Strengthen, not Load" (replaces BUILD 126's version): same ten-of-twelve-mods setup, now asserting the rendered #dieActionPanel button list has no "Load" entry (Strengthen and Skip both present), plus a defence-in-depth check that a direct dieActionChooseLoad() call still converts to Strengthen rather than building a short offer.

BASELINE NOT RUN THIS SESSION: the original prompt's 400-run `--seed=1 --dieRewardRule=asBuilt --riteHeal=20 --runs=400` baseline was stopped before completing (it was mid-run when the crash above was found) and was not re-run after the fix, on the user's explicit instruction. In its place: a 20-run smoke check (`--seed=1000 --dieRewardRule=asBuilt --riteHeal=20 --runs=20`) confirmed three-act runs complete cleanly post-fix — 20/20 runs finished with zero crashes, zero console/page errors, zero illegal plays; all 20 reached act 1, 13 reached act 1's boss (median 7 loaded faces there), 5 progressed into act 2, none yet reached act 2's boss in this small sample; 0 wins (expected — 20 runs is far too small a sample to read a win rate from, and the harder acts 2/3 have never been measured before). The win rate / per-act reach counts / per-act median-loaded-faces-at-boss report the original prompt asked for from a 400-run baseline is therefore NOT available this session — runBatch()'s summary now computes and prints it (per-act runsReachingAct/runsReachingBoss/medianFacesAtBoss, from the new row.actReached column and a non-CSV row.facesAtBossByAct field), ready to run whenever the baseline itself is authorised.

MIGRATION: tests/autoplay_results.csv backed up (backups/autoplay_results.csv.bak_20260918_202442) before rewriting; all 1,616 pre-127 rows given a trailing blank in the new actReached column (verified: every one of the file's 1,621 rows has exactly 16 comma-separated fields after the migration, zero exceptions), zero rows deleted, zero values guessed.

VERIFIED: tests/mods.test.js 20/20 before and after (unchanged — no mod behaviour touched). tests/facts.test.js 45/45 before this build's edit, 45/45 after (the D-54 test rewritten in place, not added — same count).

FILES TOUCHED: tests/autoplay.js (playOneAct() extracted, playRun() loops all acts, checkGuards() pulled out of its old per-call closure, resolveDieAction()'s eligibility check, CSV_HEADER/appendCsvRow() gain actReached, runBatch()/printSummary() report per-act medians), tests/autoplay_results.csv (actReached column added, 1,616 old rows blanked, ~25 new rows appended by the smoke tests and the aborted baseline attempt), js/rendering.js (eligibleLoadModIds() factored out, dieActionChooseLoad() simplified to a defensive fallback, renderDieActionPanel()'s 'choose' step hides Load below 3 eligible), js/config.js (BUILD: 127, no FACTS line — no GAME_CONFIG constant changed), index.html (build stamp, STAGE 2.54 / BUILD 127), tests/facts.test.js (D-54 test rewritten), CLAUDE.md (this write-up, the BUILD 126 amendment and migration note), HISTORY.md (BUILD 126's migrated CURRENT SUBSTAGE text). Stage 2.55 (BUILD 128) — checkpoint 3 map. Each lane grows from five slots to eight (F16), 7 fights per act, 21 a run; also closes KI-24 and KI-25.

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 127's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the three-act bot write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

MAP: buildAct() (run-and-map.js) keeps each lane's original five slots as an exact prefix (byte-identical object shapes to the pre-128 build — every pre-existing fact/test about those five slots' HP/intent/labels is untouched) and appends three more per lane: Fight, Fight, Rite. Upper: Fight, Rite, Fight, Elite, Rite, Fight, Fight, Rite. Lower: Fight, Rite, Fight, Fight, Rite, Fight, Fight, Rite. A normal fight's enemy "type" (which of the three NORMAL_FIGHT_HP entries it uses) is a per-lane 0/1/2 rotation advanced once per normal fight in slot order (rites/the elite don't advance it) — three distinct values rotating in a fixed order can never repeat on the very next normal fight, which is what makes "no lane shows the same normal enemy twice in a row" true by construction; each normal fight's enemy now carries its own normalTypeIndex for tests/facts.test.js to assert this directly. Rewards needed no code change at all: phase-machine.js's win branch already keys off wonSlot.label (SINGLE vs ELITE) and currentSlot === 'boss', never a slot index, so the extra Fight/Rite slots are rewarded exactly like the original five automatically.

CONFIG: GAME_CONFIG.SLOTS_PER_LANE 5 -> 8; ELITE_ALLOWED_SLOT_INDICES narrowed from [3,4] to [3] (F16 now names slot 4 only, not a range); new RITE_SLOT_INDICES [1,4,7]. F16's own wording (config.js header comment) updated to match. BUILD: 128.

KI-24 (D-29): refreshInspector()'s enemy panel (rendering.js) used to hide the die column outright for a dieless (hasDie: false) enemy — every normal/opening fight, on every act alike, since the code path is act-agnostic. Now it always shows the column, rendering an empty die slot per D-29's own wording; renderDieList() was also fixed to clear a container's existing rows when handed zero faces (it used to early-return and leave a previous fight's die rows stale) rather than just skip rendering.

KI-25 (D-53): the two remaining bare "N poison" mod log lines — cards-mods.js's blight ("applied 6 poison") and virulence ("applied 3 poison, doubled to N") — now read "applied 6 stacks of poison" and "applied 3 stacks of poison, doubled to N stacks of poison", matching every other poison log line's BUILD 121 wording. Grep-confirmed these were the only two remaining bare-poison [MOD] lines.

BOT: tests/autoplay.js's playOneAct() lane traversal is now read live off gameState.run.act.upper's own length and each slot's type/label instead of a hardcoded 5-slot array, so the identical policy plays both the old 5-slot lane and the new 8-slot lane with no code change — this is what let the "before" baseline (run against an untouched pre-128 copy of the whole project, backups/ convention, so the map/config edits below couldn't contaminate it) and the "after" baseline (same seeds, same policy, against this build's own 8-slot map) be produced by the same driver. Also new: every completed fight's own round count is now tracked (not just each run's last), and each act's own boss-arrival HP is tracked per act — runBatch()'s summary reports mean rounds/fight and, per act, mean HP entering that act's own boss (the bot question this build's prompt asked).

TESTS: tests/facts.test.js's F16 test rewritten for the 8-slot shape (lane length, rite-slot labels, elite index, fight-count-per-lane); two new tests ("no lane shows the same normal enemy twice in a row", "each path through an act is 7 fights (21 a run)"); one new KI-25 test (blight/virulence log lines read "stacks of poison", and no [MOD] line anywhere states a bare "N poison" amount). 48/48 (45/45 before, 3 added). tests/mods.test.js unchanged, 20/20 — no mod behaviour touched.

Stage 2.56 (BUILD 129) — checkpoint 3 tiers. Every offerable mod and card gets a tier; card rewards and Load offers each roll a tier per choice.

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 128's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the checkpoint 3 map write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

TIERS: every real mod (cards-mods.js's gameState.config.mods entries) and every reward-pool card (gameState.config.cards entries also referenced from config.cardPool) now carries a `tier` field, one of 'common'/'uncommon'/'rare'. Mods: common Smite/Sanctuary/Penance/Blight/Offering/Anthem, uncommon Vigil/Ordain/Zeal/Elevation, rare Virulence/Fervour. Cards: common Rebuke/Censure/Vestment/Litany/Scripture/Interdict/Orison/Censer/Purge, uncommon Judgement/Reckoning/Communion/Rapture/Covenant/Retribution. Consecrate (the class anchor) and the three starters (Strike/Ward/Rite) carry no `tier` field at all — both already excluded from every offer before this build (DIE_ACTION_EXCLUDED_MOD_IDS for Consecrate; config.cardPool never held the starters), now doubly so, since eligibleLoadModIds() and the card-reward pool builder both also filter on `tier != null`.

SPLIT/PICKER: GAME_CONFIG.TIER_SPLIT (config.js) holds a [common, uncommon, rare] probability triple per reward source — fight/rite 65/30/5, elite 40/40/20, boss 0/70/30 — and GAME_CONFIG.TIER_ORDER fixes the low-to-high walk order. state.js gained three small functions: rollTier() (one weighted Math.random() roll against a split), pickFromTier() (a random still-available entry of a given tier), and pickTieredOffer(pool, splitWeights, count) — rolls `count` independent tier choices, each falling back down the tier order first (toward common) then up (toward rare) if the rolled tier's pool is empty, removing each pick from a working copy so no offer ever repeats a piece. It returns { id, rolledTier, fallback } per choice — rolledTier is what was actually rolled, kept apart from the delivered id's own tier specifically so a fallback substitution can never contaminate a sample of the rolled-tier distribution against the split (see the new seeded test below).

WIRING: rendering.js's new currentOfferTierSplit(origin) decides which split a given offer uses, off the slot the win (or rite) came from — gameState.run.currentSlot stays pointed at the just-won slot for the whole reward flow (die action screen through card reward screen alike; only advanceRun(), which runs once the flow closes, ever moves it), so it's safe to read from either screen. origin === 'rite' (dieActionOrigin) shares the 'fight' split, per spec ("fights and rites"); a null currentSlot (a test/dev tool opening a panel directly, off the map) also falls back to 'fight'. dieActionChooseLoad() and openCardRewardScreen() both replaced their old shuffle-then-slice draw with pool.map(...) + pickTieredOffer(pool, currentOfferTierSplit(dieActionOrigin), 3) — same 3-choices, no-repeats guarantee, now tier-aware.

D-54 (Load visibility): renderDieActionPanel()'s 'choose' step now shows the Load button whenever a blank face exists among faces 2-19 (gameState.die.faces.slice(1,19).some(f => f.modId === null) — faces 1/20 are the NAT_ONE/NAT_TWENTY stubs, never blank, never part of this check), with BUILD 126/127's fewer-than-3-eligible-mods gate kept underneath as a last guard (still hides Load even when a blank face exists, if the pool itself has run dry) — the existing D-54 test (ten mods pre-loaded, exactly two eligible, several faces still blank) continues to pass unchanged since the last guard is what fires there. dieActionChooseLoad()'s own defensive Strengthen-conversion fallback is untouched.

TESTS: tests/facts.test.js gained five new tests — every mod's tier (deepStrictEqual against the list above), every reward-pool card's tier, Consecrate/Strike/Ward/Rite never offered (both carry no tier, and 500 sampled Load offers / 500 sampled card rewards never include them), a seeded 10,000-choice fight-split sample (mulberry32, same seeding approach as tests/autoplay.js) landing within 2 points of 65/30/5 — measured 64.95/30.08/4.97 against target 65/30/5, well inside tolerance, with 2 fallback substitutions in 30,000 rolls logged and counted separately, not folded into the percentages — and a 2000-sample x 3-splits x 2-pools check that no offer ever repeats a piece (0 duplicates in 12,000 sampled offers). 48/48 -> 53/53. tests/mods.test.js unchanged, 20/20 — no mod behaviour touched, only tier metadata added.

VERIFIED: tests/mods.test.js 20/20 before and after. tests/facts.test.js 48/48 before this build's edits, 53/53 after.

FILES TOUCHED: js/config.js (BUILD: 129, new TIER_ORDER/TIER_SPLIT constants), js/cards-mods.js (tier field added to twelve mods and fifteen reward-pool cards), js/state.js (rollTier()/pickFromTier()/pickTieredOffer() added after shuffle()), js/rendering.js (currentOfferTierSplit() added; eligibleLoadModIds(), dieActionChooseLoad(), openCardRewardScreen() all now tier-aware; renderDieActionPanel()'s Load-button gate rewritten for the blank-face rule), tests/facts.test.js (five new tests), index.html (build stamp, STAGE 2.56 / BUILD 129), CLAUDE.md (this write-up, the new CONFIRMED WORKING one-liner), HISTORY.md (BUILD 128's migrated CURRENT SUBSTAGE text). Backup taken before any edit: a full project copy at C:/Users/figja/Die_v1_backup_2026-09-19_tiers (CLAUDE.md names no narrower whole-project backup convention, only ad hoc per-file backups/ dated snapshots, so the same whole-project-copy fallback BUILD 128 used was used again here).

Stage 2.57 (BUILD 130) — checkpoint 3 tags, plus the six new mods that need no other new engine code.

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 129's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the checkpoint 3 tiers write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

PRE-CHANGE FINDINGS (step 3, before any edit): no tag/synergy field existed anywhere in js/ (grepped tags:/synergy/tag: — one unrelated comment hit, listener-registry.js's own doc-comment use of the word "synergy"). Vigil registers its end-of-round effect the same way every other turn-scoped listener does — registerListener('END_PLAYER_TURN', ..., 'turn') inside its effect() — and it fires because runPhase(phase) (phase-machine.js) calls the generic callListeners(phase) once at the top of every phase transition, so registering on the literal phase name 'END_PLAYER_TURN' is what makes it fire, no special dispatch exists. PHASE_ORDER puts END_PLAYER_TURN before ENEMY_ROLL_PHASE/ENEMY_ACT_PHASE, so the enemy attacks after end-of-round effects resolve, not before — confirmed directly from the array, not inferred. Zeal stores its permanent growth on the triggering face's own modData.accumulatedBonus, merged via Object.assign (never replacing modData wholesale, since mod_dispatch already wrote this trigger's own triggerCount into it first) — this is ARCH-CF2 in practice, and Cope below copies the pattern verbatim under its own field name. tests/autoplay.js does keep its own mod list — LOAD_PRIORITY, a flat array of the twelve previously-loadable mod ids in Fergus's rank order — checked against the live loadable set by assertEveryLoadableModIsRanked()/facts.test.js's "LOAD_PRIORITY ranks every loadable mod exactly once" test, so it had to be extended or that test would fail.

TAGS: every mod (cards-mods.js's gameState.config.mods entries) and every card (gameState.config.cards entries, cardPool included) now carries a `tags` field, an array of zero or more words — Poison: Blight/Virulence/Censer/Purge/Reckoning; Mass: Fervour/Elevation/Zeal/Ordain/Anthem/Covenant; Growth: Zeal/Elevation/Ordain (a piece can hold both Mass and Growth, e.g. Zeal); Soul: Consecrate/Offering/Communion/Rapture; Bastion: Sanctuary/Penance/Vigil/Retribution; every other existing piece (Smite, Judgement, the three starters, etc.) gets `[]`.

SIX NEW MODS (all added to gameState.config.mods in cards-mods.js's init(), right after Elevation, before renderDevModOptions()): Largesse (common, ['soul']) — +2 soul, 4 block, flat, same two existing paths Consecrate's soul grant and Ward's block already use. Tithe (uncommon, ['soul']) — registers on the exact same generic 'END_PLAYER_TURN' hook Vigil uses (same turn-scoped registerListener()/clearListeners('turn') pattern, own listener id so it coexists with Vigil/Anathema independently); at end of round, 5 damage per soul remaining, capped at 20 (Math.min-style two-branch cap, same style as Retribution's own block cap), routed through dealDamage() tagged 'attack' so Fervour's DAMAGE_MULTIPLIER doubles it like any other attack mod. Congregation (uncommon, ['growth']) — 8 damage, 16 if another loaded mod anywhere on the die (either slot) carries the Growth tag; "never counts itself" falls out for free rather than needing bookkeeping, since eligibleLoadModIds() (rendering.js) already refuses to offer a mod already loaded anywhere on the die, so at most one Congregation can ever be loaded — excluding modId/modId2 === 'congregation' from the scan is sufficient. Cope (common, ['growth','bastion']) — 8 block, permanently +2 per trigger, stored in the triggering face's own modData.copeBonus, merged via Object.assign exactly the way Zeal's accumulatedBonus already is (ARCH-CF2); writes no weight anywhere (ARCH-CF6 holds trivially — nothing to check). Anathema (uncommon, ['bastion']) — same generic END_PLAYER_TURN hook as Tithe/Vigil, own listener id; at end of round, damage equal to current block, capped at 16; block is read only (gameState.player.block), never modified — still clears at the next START_OF_TURN exactly as always, unaffected by this mod. Thurible (common, ['poison']) — 8 damage, applies 3 stacks of poison via the existing poisonStacks field every other poison source already writes to; its log line reads "applied 3 stacks of poison" from the moment it was written, per KI-25's established wording, never a bare "N poison" amount.

MOD COUNT (F24): 13 -> 19. Updated in three places, none trusted from the others — js/config.js's header FACTS comment (line-by-line, now "F24 mods 19 (checkpoint 3 tags/mods build: 6 new — ...)"), CLAUDE.md's own FACTS record (this file), and tests/facts.test.js's own three separate hardcoded assertions: the F24 test itself (renamed "F24 mods 19", asserts Object.keys(gameState.config.mods).length === 19), the BUILD 118 "a mod on no face records zero" test's own count check (collectTriggerCountsByMod() must return exactly 19 keys), and the BUILD 117 "every mod has a hover description" test's own count check (MOD_DESCRIPTION, rendering.js, gained all six new entries so `missing` stays empty). GAME_CONFIG.BUILD bumped 129 -> 130.

D-54 TEST FIX (a consequence of the mod count changing, not a new decision): the existing D-54 test pre-loaded ten of the (then) twelve loadable mods to leave exactly two (penance, vigil) unloaded; with eighteen loadable mods now, ten-loaded would leave eight, not two, so it now pre-loads sixteen (the twelve original loadable mods plus all six new ones) across sixteen blank faces, still leaving exactly penance and vigil unloaded — same assertions, same intent, just resized for the new pool.

LOAD OFFER POOL / TESTS/AUTOPLAY.JS: all six new mods are ordinary gameState.config.mods entries with a `tier` field (Largesse/Cope/Thurible common; Tithe/Congregation/Anathema uncommon), so they flow through the existing checkpoint-3-tiers Load machinery with no further wiring. tests/autoplay.js's own LOAD_PRIORITY list (its private mod-ranking copy, confirmed to exist in step 3) had the six new ids appended at the end, unranked among themselves relative to Fergus's original 1-12 order — no rank pick exists for them yet, and assertEveryLoadableModIsRanked() only requires every loadable mod appear exactly once, not in any particular position. Not run this session, per instruction — a static edit only.

TESTS: tests/mods.test.js gained six new per-mod tests (Largesse, Tithe [uncapped + capped], Congregation [baseline + boosted, proving it never counts itself], Cope [modData growth], Anathema [uncapped + capped, block unspent], Thurible [damage + poison + log wording]) — 20/20 -> 26/26. tests/facts.test.js gained ten new tests: every mod's tags (deepStrictEqual against the full list above), every reward-pool card's tags, the three starters' empty tags, one behavioural test per new mod checking its numbers and cap, and a Load-offerability spot-check (all six appear in Load offers within 1000 samples; ARCH-CF6 noted as holding by inspection — none of the six write face weight at all) — plus the F24/D-54 count fixes above — 53/53 -> 63/63.

Stage 2.59 (BUILD 132) — checkpoint 3, trigger a face outside a roll (prompt D). One shared function (triggerFaceOutsideRoll(), pipeline.js), then the three pieces that use it: Threnody, Reverberation, Magnificat.

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 130's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the checkpoint 3 tags/six-mods write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

PRE-CHANGE FINDINGS (step 3, before any edit): a rolled face's mods trigger via resolvePlayerRoll() (pipeline.js) — for a loaded face it dispatches callListeners('MOD_TRIGGER', { modId: face.modId, faceNumber }), then, if face.modId2 is set, a second sequential callListeners('MOD_TRIGGER', { modId: face.modId2, faceNumber }) — modId always before modId2, load order, each resolving fully (its effect() call returns) before the next begins (BUILD 115). A blank face (both modId slots null) instead dispatches callListeners('BLANK_ROLL', {}), which reaches the Ordained's own onBlankRoll passive (cards-mods.js, registered once in init()) — generateBlock(GAME_CONFIG.BLANK_ROLL_BLOCK) via dealBlock(2, 'blank_face'), currently 2 block (F05). Trigger counts are incremented inside mod_dispatch, the one permanent 'MOD_TRIGGER' listener (cards-mods.js): it reads data.faceNumber to find the triggering face, bumps modData.triggerCount (if data.modId matches face.modId) or modData.triggerCount2 (if it matches face.modId2), merging into any existing modData via Object.assign rather than replacing it wholesale (ARCH-CF2) — this is the same funnel a normal roll, Nat 20's onNatTwenty() loop, and this build's new outside-roll function all share; nothing bumps a trigger count anywhere else. Grepped D-51 and any existing round-scoped trigger cap across js/ and CLAUDE.md — no hit: no per-round trigger cap of any kind existed in code before this build. tests/autoplay.js does keep its own mod list — LOAD_PRIORITY, a flat array of loadable mod ids — so Magnificat had to be appended to it (at the end, unranked, per instruction, not run this session).

SHARED FUNCTION: triggerFaceOutsideRoll(faceNumber) (pipeline.js, new, placed directly after resolvePlayerRoll()) is the one function every "trigger a face without rolling it" card/mod goes through. Refuses outright (returns false, no state change) for face 1 or face GAME_CONFIG.DIE_SIZE.PLAYER (20) — both are Nat stubs, never a real mod or a blank, and the prompt names them undoable. Refuses a face already triggered this way once this round via a new round-scoped record, gameState.turn.outsideTriggeredFaces (state.js, an array of face numbers), cleared to [] at START_OF_TURN (phase-machine.js) alongside every other per-round roll flag already cleared there — the same single clear site, not a second one. Refuses once gameState.turn.roundTriggerCount reaches GAME_CONFIG.ROUND_TRIGGER_CAP (D-51, new — no cap existed in code before this build, so a round-scoped counter capped at 10 was added, exactly as the prompt's fallback instructs). A loaded face's own trigger goes through the identical MOD_TRIGGER dispatch a rolled face uses (mod_dispatch), both slots, load order — permanent growth (Zeal's accumulatedBonus, Ordain's/Elevation's weight write via strengthenFace(), Cope's copeBonus) runs exactly as it would on a roll, since it lives inside each mod's own effect(), reached identically either way; a blank face dispatches BLANK_ROLL for the same 2 block. Never touches gameState.turn.rolledFaceNumber/rollOutcome/rolledFaceWeight — does not change which face was rolled this round, per the prompt.

D-51 TRIGGER CAP: GAME_CONFIG.ROUND_TRIGGER_CAP = 10 (config.js, new). gameState.turn.roundTriggerCount (state.js, new) counts every real MOD_TRIGGER dispatch — mod_dispatch (cards-mods.js) increments it on every call unless data.natTwentySweep is true — with the one exemption the prompt names: onNatTwenty()'s own sweep (cards-mods.js) now tags both of its callListeners('MOD_TRIGGER', ...) calls with natTwentySweep: true, so a Nat 20 with many loaded faces is never blocked by the cap. triggerFaceOutsideRoll()'s blank branch bumps the same counter directly, since BLANK_ROLL bypasses mod_dispatch entirely and would otherwise not count toward the cap at all. Cleared to 0 at START_OF_TURN, same site as outsideTriggeredFaces above.

THRENODY: card, tier uncommon, cost 2, tags Growth. Scans gameState.die.faces for every loaded face (modId !== null, excluding the two Nat stubs), picks the lowest-numbered one, and calls triggerFaceOutsideRoll() on it. No loaded face at all logs and does nothing (unreachable in real play, since Consecrate always occupies face 10 from run start, but handled rather than left to throw).

REVERBERATION: card, tier rare, cost 2, tags Mass. Reads gameState.turn.rolledFaceNumber (the same field Covenant/Tabernacle already read) and re-triggers that exact face via triggerFaceOutsideRoll() — a loaded face triggers again, a blank face gives its 2 block again. Faces 1 and 20 are Nat stubs triggerFaceOutsideRoll() always refuses, so a Nat roll is special-cased ahead of that call: 6 block, direct, via dealBlock(), per the prompt's own "on a 1 or a 20, 6 block instead."

MAGNIFICAT: mod, tier rare, tags Mass. On trigger, scans every OTHER loaded face on the die (excluding its own face, data.faceNumber, and the two Nat stubs) for the heaviest weight; ties go to the lowest-numbered face (a single reduce() comparing weight first, then face number). Triggers the winner through triggerFaceOutsideRoll(). No other loaded face at all logs and does nothing. Added to gameState.config.mods in cards-mods.js's init(), right after Thurible; flows through the existing checkpoint-3-tiers Load machinery via its own `tier` field, no further wiring (eligibleLoadModIds() already filters on `tier != null`). Appended to tests/autoplay.js's LOAD_PRIORITY at the end, not run this session.

COUNTS (F24/F25): mods 19 -> 20 (Magnificat), cards 31 -> 33 (Threnody, Reverberation). Updated in js/config.js's header FACTS comment (F24/F25 lines) and every place tests/facts.test.js hardcodes either count: the F24/F25 tests themselves, the BUILD 118 "a mod on no face records zero" test's collectTriggerCountsByMod() key-count check, the BUILD 117 "every mod has a hover description" test's own total check, the D-54 test (resized from sixteen pre-loaded mods across sixteen faces to seventeen across seventeen, since Magnificat is now a nineteenth loadable mod, still leaving exactly penance and vigil unloaded), and both BUILD 129 tier maps and both BUILD 130 tags maps (magnificat/threnody/reverberation entries added to each). GAME_CONFIG.BUILD bumped 131 -> 132.

TESTS: tests/mods.test.js gained four new tests — Magnificat (heaviest-other-face selection, the weight tie-break, and that a lighter loaded face is never picked, all in one pass), and three engine-level tests calling triggerFaceOutsideRoll() directly: refuses face 1 and face 20 (and confirms face 1's refusal never actually armed Penitence), refuses a second outside trigger of the same face in one round, and stops at GAME_CONFIG.ROUND_TRIGGER_CAP (eleven single-mod faces attempted, exactly ten succeed) — 26/26 -> 30/30. tests/facts.test.js gained six new tests — Threnody (lowest-numbered loaded face), Reverberation (a loaded face re-triggers, a blank face's 2 block repeats, a Nat roll gives 6 block instead — tested via a Nat 1 roll rather than Nat 20, since a Nat 20 sweep would also trigger the anchor's own Consecrate, whose ON_CARD_PLAY passive would then fire again when Reverberation itself is played and confound the block delta), and a Magnificat Load-offerability spot-check (ARCH-CF6 holds by inspection — it never writes face.weight, only reads it off the candidates it scans) — plus the count/tier/tags map updates above — 81/81 -> 86/86.

Stage 2.60 (BUILD 133) — checkpoint 3, the Bound engine: a die-wide Bound scan, a fight-scoped Bound setter, a fast sweep, and three plain Bound mods (Unison, Accord, Kinship).

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 132's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the outside-roll-trigger/Threnody/Reverberation/Magnificat write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

PRE-CHANGE FINDINGS (step 3, before any edit): the outside-roll trigger function is triggerFaceOutsideRoll(faceNumber) (pipeline.js) — already built at BUILD 132, unchanged by this build except that it's now also what the Bound scan calls. Grepped for any existing per-trigger delay on the Nat 20 sweep — none exists: onNatTwenty() (cards-mods.js) dispatched every loaded face's MOD_TRIGGER synchronously, back to back, in the same forEach, with no setTimeout/pacing anywhere in the engine or in audio.js's own mod_trigger sound (which only shifts pitch per chain step, never timing) — a Nat 20 sweep played every trigger in one instant tick before this build. Fight-scoped state is cleared at fight end by clearFightScopedState() (run-and-map.js), shared by resetFight() (dev Restart Fight) and every real fight-to-fight transition (beginFightFromSlot()/advanceRun()) — this is where the new Bound grant's own clear was added. tests/autoplay.js does keep its own mod list (LOAD_PRIORITY), so Unison/Accord/Kinship were appended to it, at the end, not run this session.

BOUND DEFINITION: isBoundFace(face) (pipeline.js, new) — a face is Bound if a mod loaded on it (either slot) carries the 'bound' tag (printed Bound, permanent), or if face.modData.boundGranted is set (granted for the fight). See BOUND ENGINE (new standing section) for the full mechanic.

SETTER: grantBoundToFace(faceNumber) (pipeline.js, new) grants Bound to a loaded face for the rest of the current fight, merged into that face's own modData (ARCH-CF2 — same Object.assign merge pattern Zeal/Cope already use, never replacing modData wholesale). Refuses outright (no state change, returns false) for face 1, face GAME_CONFIG.DIE_SIZE.PLAYER (20), and a genuinely blank face (both slots null).

SCAN: runBoundScan(rolledFace) (pipeline.js, new), called only from resolvePlayerRoll()'s own mod-trigger branch (never from onNatTwenty()'s Nat 20 branch, so the scan structurally cannot run during a Nat 20 sweep). When the rolled face is itself Bound, every OTHER loaded Bound face on the die triggers through triggerFaceOutsideRoll(), ascending face order (gameState.die.faces is already stored ascending, so filtering preserves order), any position on the die. A face triggered this way never starts a further scan — triggerFaceOutsideRoll() has no notion of what called it, so nothing recurses.

FAST SWEEP (timing only, no layout change, no other numbers changed): playSweep(faceNumbers, dispatchFn) (pipeline.js, new) paces WHEN each face in a multi-face sweep plays — every dispatch still runs its full effect the instant its own setTimeout fires, so state updates are unaffected, only their wall-clock spacing. gameState.turn.roundSweepPlays (state.js, new) counts every sweep-played trigger this round, both Nat 20 sweeps and Bound scans alike, cleared at START_OF_TURN alongside every other round-scoped flag. The first three plays in a round land GAME_CONFIG.SWEEP_TRIGGER_DELAY_MS (200ms, new) apart; every play after the third lands at a quarter of that delay (50ms) instead. onNatTwenty() (cards-mods.js) and runBoundScan() (pipeline.js) both now dispatch through playSweep() instead of a plain forEach.

THREE PLAIN BOUND MODS (all added to gameState.config.mods in cards-mods.js's init(), right after Magnificat, before renderDevModOptions()): Unison (common, ['bound']) — 6 damage, flat, no conditions. Accord (common, ['bound']) — 10 block, flat, no conditions. Kinship (common, ['bound', 'poison']) — applies 4 stacks of poison to the enemy, same poisonStacks field every other poison source already writes to.

COUNTS (F24): mods 20 -> 23 (Unison, Accord, Kinship). Updated in js/config.js's header FACTS comment (F24 line) and every place tests/facts.test.js hardcodes the mod count: the F24 test itself, the BUILD 118 "a mod on no face records zero" test's collectTriggerCountsByMod() key-count check, the BUILD 117 "every mod has a hover description" test's own total check, the D-54 test (resized from seventeen pre-loaded mods across seventeen faces to twenty across the same seventeen faces — three of them now also carrying a modId2 — since Unison/Accord/Kinship make twenty-two loadable mods, still leaving exactly penance and vigil unloaded), and both BUILD 129/130 tier and tags maps (unison/accord/kinship entries added to each). F25 (cards) is unchanged — this build adds no cards. CLAUDE.md itself carries no separate copy of the FACTS text (only config.js's header and the Notion "Die — V1" page do, per PROJECT's own description) — the Notion page was not reached this session (no MCP access in scope for this task) and was not updated; flagged, not silently skipped, same as BUILD 131/132's own findings. Stage 2.61 (BUILD 134) — checkpoint 3, the remaining Bound pieces: Kyrie, Novena, Canticle (cards), Concord and Herald (mods), and Herald's random target pick. Closes checkpoint 3.

MIGRATED FIRST: BUILD 133's own CURRENT SUBSTAGE (the Bound engine write-up, in full) moved to HISTORY.md verbatim before this section was overwritten.

PRE-CHANGE FINDINGS (step 3, before any edit, as the session required):
- Unison prints Bound by carrying the 'bound' tag; MOD_DESCRIPTION (rendering.js) appends the literal string ". Bound" to its base effect text — `unison: '6 damage. Bound'` — no separate "Bound" flag or renderer, just a suffix on the hover string. Concord and Herald follow the identical convention.
- The fight-scoped Bound setter is grantBoundToFace(faceNumber) (pipeline.js, BUILD 133) — merges `{ modData: { boundGranted: true } }` into a face, refusing face 1/face 20/a blank face; cleared at fight end by clearFightScopedState() (run-and-map.js).
- The outside-roll trigger function is triggerFaceOutsideRoll(faceNumber) (pipeline.js, BUILD 132) — unchanged by this build, reused by Novena exactly as Threnody/Reverberation/Magnificat already reuse it, paced through playSweep() (pipeline.js, BUILD 133) for the multi-face case, same as runBoundScan().
- The game's random source is still bare Math.random(), used through shuffle() (state.js) — grep-confirmed the only Math.random() call sites remain shuffle(), rollTier()/pickFromTier() (state.js), rollDie() (pipeline.js), and the enemy intent roll (phase-machine.js); no new source was introduced.
- tests/autoplay.js does keep its own mod list (LOAD_PRIORITY) — Concord and Herald were appended to its end, not run this session, per instruction.

RANDOM PICK: pickRandom(list) (state.js, new) — `list[Math.floor(Math.random() * list.length)]`, the same Math.random() source shuffle()/rollTier()/pickFromTier() already use; returns undefined for an empty list rather than throwing. Used only by Herald so far.

THE THREE CARDS (cards-mods.js, added just before config.cardPool, alongside Threnody/Reverberation): Kyrie (common, cost 1, tags ['bound']) — 5 damage, 10 if isBoundFace() on the rolled face (gameState.turn.rolledFaceNumber) is true. Novena (rare, cost 2, tags ['bound']) — every loaded Bound face on the die (isBoundFace(), excluding Nat faces) triggers, each through triggerFaceOutsideRoll(), paced by playSweep() the same way runBoundScan() already sweeps multiple faces; a no-loaded-Bound-face roll is a no-op, logged. Canticle (uncommon, cost 1, tags ['bound']) — 6 block; if the rolled face is loaded (a real mod, not a Nat stub), it's granted Bound for the fight via grantBoundToFace(); a Nat roll is never "loaded" by this check, and grantBoundToFace() itself would refuse it anyway — belt and suspenders, matching the prompt's explicit "never face 1 or 20." All three added to config.cardPool alongside threnody/reverberation.

THE TWO MODS (cards-mods.js, added right after Kinship, before renderDevModOptions()): Concord (uncommon, tags ['bound', 'soul']) — +1 soul, 3 block, same shape as Largesse's own soul+block combo. Herald (rare, tags ['bound']) — 6 damage; among the die's other loaded, non-Nat, non-own faces that are NOT already Bound, one is picked at random via pickRandom() and granted Bound for the fight via grantBoundToFace(); no eligible candidate is a no-op, only the damage happens. Candidates deliberately include the anchor (Consecrate, face 10) whenever it's loaded and not Bound — it's just another loaded, non-Bound face from Herald's own point of view, nothing special-cased.
